PROGRAM CHARTER 00

Executive contract and present-state truth

This dossier specifies the technical work required to bring EXO ClothCraft/EXO Sample 3D toward a reliable CLO-class garment CAD, simulation and production workflow. It is a proposed implementation contract. It does not claim that EXO has achieved exact CLO equivalence, certified apparel fitting or production-approved manufacturing output.

Evidence dimensionVerified repository snapshotRemaining validation
Native toolbar mapping70 observed CLO 7.0.242 commands; 11 native / 9 inspect / 50 unavailableFunctional click → model edit → undo → reopen → 2D/3D matching for each slot
Fabric library400 estimated specs, 405 Rust choices; 385 photo-linked visual variants400 genuine calibrated tests are NOT present; per fabric optical source matching and drape validation
Physical optics67 installed CC0 source materials; 44 referenced; 201 map imagesSource construction identity, centimeters-per-tile, scene color matching and yarn-scale validation
Preview dynamicsReact PBD, Rust VBD, C++ XPBD and Rust PPF paths existSingle canonical project contract, confidence levels and cloth/body swept collision evidence
ManufacturingDXF/POM/BOM/marker/factory ZIP generatorsIndependent third-party import, actual cutting, physical samples and signed package acceptance
DesktopSigned WebKit app and developing Qt/C++ structuresFull authoring interaction evidence and cross-platform packaging

Priority rule: preserve truthful user-facing capability indicators. A button in the toolbar, a data record or a screenshot does not prove its semantic operation. Each following work item has an acceptance boundary and source pointers. "Future" sections are optional architectural extensions rather than blockers for core CAD.

Source-of-truth audit provenance: repository at /blender-mac-workspace/exo-clothcraft-v2; current working tree contains active uncommitted changes. Evidence labels below are snapshots, not reproducible build green statuses. The PDF/Docx themselves are planning artifacts and do not modify EXO code.

docs/EXO_MASTER_ROADMAP.md; docs/EXO_RELEASE_STATUS.mdEXO ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION · 16 OCT 2026
PROGRAM EXECUTION 01

Dependency-critical implementation order

SequenceProgram gateBlocking evidence
M0Canonical schema, deterministic builds, versioned operation contractsClean native builds, migration and fixture corpus
M12D CAD + toolbar/property semantic correctness70-slot matrix, exact tools, cross-revision undo
M2Seam topology and 2D↔3D correspondenceSewing, arrangement, stable mesh/source mapping
M3Cloth physical solver, avatar, swept contactAccepted multi-frame tee/pants/layer tests
M4Calibrated fabrics and truthful PBRSpecimen source and optical/mechanical independent evidence
M5Fit insights + production measurementsSigned landmarks, explanatory heat maps
M6Grading, markers, factory interoperabilityThird-party CAD DXF roundtrip and cut-test
M7Signed desktop, QA, scripting, open-source packagingClean machine smoke, key control e2e tests
M8Rendering, asset libraries, photographyColor managed look and artifact provenance
M9+Community, tech packs, spatial optional productsAuthentication, permissions, immutable approved releases

Gate transition rule

No issue advances because its file exists or a test passes in only one layer. A gate requires the exact fixture revision, native build hash, automated assertions, adversarial failure test and an inspection report. Each completion ticket must identify a responsible implementation surface and a separate reviewer.

Suggested issue fields

Each ticket: SPEC-ID, feature, source location, acceptance date, current evidence class, blocking prerequisites, exact user workflow, input/output schema, undo state, expected and failure behavior, screenshot/regression path, QA owner and supporting measurement evidence.

docs/EXO_MASTER_ROADMAP.md; docs/EXO_VALIDATION_TEST_PLAN.mdEXO ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION · 16 OCT 2026
REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE 02

System boundaries and canonical dependency graph

Local EXO authoring owns private garment source, edits and physical models. The React/WebKit shell displays 2D/3D state; Rust V2 powers selected live native commands; the C++ CAD library contains a broad but separately verified conversion set; PPF remains a bounded optional solver service. Future community and manufacturer services receive only creator-authorized immutable publish packages, never access to arbitrary local workspace files.

LayerOwnsReceivesNever assume
UItool state, viewport state, temporary selectionstable document snapshots, tool capabilitiesUI event alone mutates native model
Project documentIDs, units, 2D pieces, seams, fabric, trim, revisionsatomic commands, migrationmesh vertex indexes are durable IDs
Geometry/CAD2D curves, topology, seam pairing, meshing, 2D/3D mapauthoring graphcut boundary is equivalent to stitch edge
Physicsmaterial-specific constraints, avatar contact, time trajectoriesvalidated mesh and constitutive calibrationfinite run means valid garment fit
VisualsPBR textures, masks, studio lights, still/video rendersUVs, optical material profilesvisual wash changes fabric mechanics
Factorygrade, POM/BOM, markers, DXF release bundleapproved document revisionexport ZIP passes cutter acceptance
Platformauthorized published derivatives, entitlement, proposalsexplicit immutable creator releasepublic preview grants source rights

Recommended data path: user command → precondition and expected revision → document mutation → dependency invalidation → deterministic derived geometry and meshes → selected solver with diagnostics → rendering/inspection → production preflight → authorized immutable export. Persist named transformations, units and hashes at every boundary; record backend identity alongside output.

Do not conflate C++ export-count parity or Rust available commands with finished GUI integration. Errors from a stale build directory or broken test selector are evidence about test setup and should be distinguished from numerical test failures.

docs/EXO_ARCHITECTURE.md; docs/EXO_ECOSYSTEM_MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRAM_2026-10-16.mdEXO ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION · 16 OCT 2026
INDEPENDENT WORKER AUDIT EVIDENCE-01

Latest known validation gaps and confidence boundaries

The current 16 October worker audit reviewed active Rust V2 and the separate C++ garment CAD and solver modules. These findings supersede optimistic readings of older parity reports but do not erase previously implemented features. They are source-level evidence and targeted test attempts, not blanket statements about future builds.

BoundarySource-level evidenceRemaining work
Rust V2 authoringexo-session.rs has edit, undo/redo, set-property, line-spec, pose/pull/grab, step and saveUnify with canonical geometry/project metadata, native command actions and property persistence tests
Fit badgeV2 snapshot explicitly carries fitCertified:falseOnly promote per verified trajectory and material/pose fixture
C++ conversionDated status reports 175 of 182 JS mappings (96.2%) and previous 58/58 parity scriptsExport mappings ≠ working full desktop/CLO product; retest from current clean checkout
C++ collision parityHistorical collision deviations around 0.074m tee and 0.055m pants RMSFix coupled contact and swept checks before any physically validated fit claim
Manufacturing byte agreementReported 16/24 tee and 20/24 pants factory ZIP file byte matchesInvestigate all divergent metadata and content; independently import exported DXF
Qt native UIOptional Qt shell and CAD core; native Qt simulation action not fully wiredIntegrate actual command path, renderer, property controls and signed app testing
Fresh native suite58 tests NOT RUN in attempted existing build cache pointing to retired checkout pathClean out-of-tree CMake configuration, build and new CTest evidence; NOT RUN is not 58 failed assertions

Source references: native_cpp/CONVERSION_STATUS_2026-10-15.md; integrations/clothcraft/exo-session.rs; native_cpp/core/cloth_collision_step.cpp; native_cpp/desktop; native_cpp/tests. Any numerical value in this page must be tied to its dated fixture rather than generalized to every garment, pose, or backend.

A durable program dashboard should attach code revision, hardware, software versions, build flags and fixtures to every result. The same codebase may have a UI capability, an unrelated library function and a separate solver experiment; give each its own verification state.

EXO ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION · 16 OCT 2026
SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-001

Canonical garment document

Objective / acceptance scope

Single revisioned source of truth for 2D pattern, 3D arrangement, seam graph, fabric assignments, avatar pose and exports

Actual source locations

src/core/project.js; src/core/backends/protocol.js; native_cpp/core/garment_document.hpp

Implementation requirements

Define versioned Project/Pattern/Edge/Curve/Seam/Fabric/Avatar/Trim/Manufacturing models; immutable IDs; unit scale; coordinate frames; provenance; explicit migrations. Convert native Rust session snapshots and C++ document snapshots through a schema-preserving boundary.

Proposed typed contract

ProjectRevision{id,revision,parents,units,hash,entities,invalidations}; no data-derived element ID may silently shift on edit.

Required verification and failure cases

Save/edit/undo/reopen/re-export consistency on tee, pants and jacket; reject stale revision and dangling foreign key without modifying disk

ExecutionDependency and integrationDefinition of done
M0/P0Source-model revision guard, source IDs and explicit invalidation of dependent cachesNamed QA evidence, reproducible fixture, snapshot, proper error state
Integration ownershipBrowser/React view, signed Rust V2 action, separate C++/PPF service as applicableDo not declare implemented until actual active workspace path uses new semantics
Rollback and compatibilityMigration route for old native/browser documents, inverse command or snapshotSave/reopen/undo and alternate-backend non-regression

The implementation must not claim commercially certified CLO parity simply because a visual analogue exists. Deliver acceptance evidence with numerical physical units and a human-reviewable before/after record. Persist unsupported reasons until tested rather than mapping a reference toolbar action to an unrelated operation.

src/core/project.js; src/core/backends/protocol.js; native_cpp/core/garment_document.hppEXO ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION · 16 OCT 2026
SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-002

Typed command engine and undo

Objective / acceptance scope

Make every toolbar action a deterministic, testable edit with proper cancellation and history

Actual source locations

src/core/workspaceCommandIndex.js; src/core/cloPropertyCommands.js; integrations/clothcraft/exo-session.rs

Implementation requirements

Register typed operations with target preconditions, dry-run diff, effect scopes, inverse snapshot and atomic commit. Choose one transaction owner per change and map browser/native bindings to same command semantics.

Proposed typed contract

CommandEnvelope{commandId,targetIds,params,expectedRevision,selection,units}; CommandResult{revision,createdIds,changedIds,invalidated,errors}.

Required verification and failure cases

Bad inputs leave project byte-identical; grouped drag is one history step; undo/redo survives native restart and invalidates dependent projections

ExecutionDependency and integrationDefinition of done
M0/P0Source-model revision guard, source IDs and explicit invalidation of dependent cachesNamed QA evidence, reproducible fixture, snapshot, proper error state
Integration ownershipBrowser/React view, signed Rust V2 action, separate C++/PPF service as applicableDo not declare implemented until actual active workspace path uses new semantics
Rollback and compatibilityMigration route for old native/browser documents, inverse command or snapshotSave/reopen/undo and alternate-backend non-regression

The implementation must not claim commercially certified CLO parity simply because a visual analogue exists. Deliver acceptance evidence with numerical physical units and a human-reviewable before/after record. Persist unsupported reasons until tested rather than mapping a reference toolbar action to an unrelated operation.

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-003

2D planar geometry kernel

Objective / acceptance scope

Exact point, curve, segment and topology operations without pixel-space drift

Actual source locations

src/core/draftingPrecision.js; src/core/draftingTopology.js; native_cpp/core/drafting_precision.cpp

Implementation requirements

Use stable edge-segment IDs and declared units. Evaluate Bézier derivatives and arc length; robust intersection predicates; exact decimal input; split/reparameterize edges while preserving attachment parameters.

Proposed typed contract

CurveEdge{id,from,to,kind,controls,orientation,arcLengthTolerance}; tolerance policy defines mm to internal coordinate conversion.

Required verification and failure cases

Golden analytic geometry fixtures at many zoom/DPR levels; no inverted winding or dangling notch after split; no self intersection accepted silently

ExecutionDependency and integrationDefinition of done
M1/P0Source-model revision guard, source IDs and explicit invalidation of dependent cachesNamed QA evidence, reproducible fixture, snapshot, proper error state
Integration ownershipBrowser/React view, signed Rust V2 action, separate C++/PPF service as applicableDo not declare implemented until actual active workspace path uses new semantics

The implementation must not claim commercially certified CLO parity simply because a visual analogue exists. Deliver acceptance evidence with numerical physical units and a human-reviewable before/after record. Persist unsupported reasons until tested rather than mapping a reference toolbar action to an unrelated operation.

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-004

2D drawing and selection

Objective / acceptance scope

Selection, lasso, snap, node edits, keyboard precision and viewport state

Actual source locations

src/components/PatternEditor.jsx; src/core/draftingSnapping.js

Implementation requirements

Specify pointer hit-test order, snapping priorities, selection cycling, drag preview commit, keyboard nudges, multi-select and focused command routing. Keep screen transforms separate from physical points.

Proposed typed contract

SelectionState{kind,ids,active,mode}; DragPreview{baseRevision,proposedGeometry,precisionMm}.

Required verification and failure cases

Pointer and keyboard produce equivalent geometry; viewport zoom does not alter final cm measurements

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-005

2D drafting constructions

Objective / acceptance scope

Native darts, slash/spread, pleat, fullness, mirror, unfold and linked pieces

Actual source locations

src/core/draftingConstructions.js; src/core/draftingUnfold.js; native_cpp/core/drafting_constructions.cpp

Implementation requirements

Model fold/dart legs, apex, take-up and dependent constraints. Use constructive graph nodes whose control points remain editable; reconstruct boundary stitching, cut offsets and annotations after each operation.

Proposed typed contract

Construction{id,type,parentPiece,anchors,parameters,dependentEdges,revision}.

Required verification and failure cases

Known dart intake and fold tests, mirrored tangent continuity and seam lengths preserved under undo/export/import

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-006

Notches and seam allowance CAD

Objective / acceptance scope

Cut-line offsets, variable width, corners, notches and seam line differentiation

Actual source locations

src/core/draftingPrecision.js; src/core/productionPreflight.js

Implementation requirements

Compute offset curves with robust join semantics, corner trimming and collision checks. Distinguish stitch line, cut line, turn/fold line and metadata references. Annotate cut marks with consistent scale.

Proposed typed contract

Allowance{edgeId,widthMm,cornerPolicy,lockMode}; Notch{edgeId,t,depthMm,style,gradeMode}.

Required verification and failure cases

1:1 paper plot measurements and independently checked DXF layer output; no allowance becomes a sewing edge by accident

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-007

Linked 2D to 3D correspondence

Objective / acceptance scope

Lossless mapping of every authored piece and semantic line to render/solve mesh

Actual source locations

src/core/clothMeshing.js; src/core/liveDrapeTransfer.js; native_cpp/core/pattern_mesh.cpp

Implementation requirements

Maintain source vertex and edge-param lineage during triangulation and remeshing; define index stable until invalidated. Propagate UVs, grainline, material IDs and seam constraints.

Proposed typed contract

MeshMapping{pieceId,meshVersion,vertexSources,edgeRanges,triangleSources,uvFrame}.

Required verification and failure cases

Highlighting a source line finds correct 3D surface after subdivision; editing point invalidates only dependent solve caches

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-008

Sewing graph and partial seams

Objective / acceptance scope

Segment/free sewing, orientation, lengths, easing, seam types and topology diagnostics

Actual source locations

src/core/sewingConstruction.js; src/core/backends/seamAssembly.js; native_cpp/core/native_seam_assembly.cpp

Implementation requirements

Treat seam as a pair of oriented parameter intervals on persistent boundary edges. Build stitch samples by arc-length correspondence, reconcile different discretizations and detect gaps or duplicates.

Proposed typed contract

Seam{id,left:{edge,t0,t1},right:{edge,t0,t1},direction,ease,stitch,allowance}.

Required verification and failure cases

Full/partial seam fixtures, reverse-edge direction, oversized ease ratio rejection, seam walk report and undo/redo reopening

SYSTEM WORK PACKAGE ARCH-011

Avatar geometry and measurement

Objective / acceptance scope

Body collision mesh, pose rig, standardized landmarks, shape and anthropometric control

Actual source locations

src/core/meshAvatarContact.js; public/avatars; blender_bridge

Implementation requirements

3D VIEW / COLUMN A / NATIVE TOOL-001

Simulate

Observed CLO7 slotCategoryEXO Rust V2 statusCurrent native route
3D · A · A01Workspace / simulationNATIVEsimulation.toggle

Observed purpose and target user action

Simulate is a separately named control in the 3D reference viewport. Exact interaction contracts, flyout variants and selection preconditions must be confirmed against the saved CLO 7.0.242 reference session. In EXO, selection must resolve to the expected authored object kind for workspace / simulation; an active icon or cursor alone is insufficient.

Required native implementation

Validate current state and command preconditions, expose real operation only when backend implements it and return typed state change with revision and diagnostics.

Data and transaction contract

CommandEnvelope and ProjectRevision. Use stable object identifiers, physical millimeter/centimeter units where geometry is involved, explicit typed parameters and expectedRevision. The command must return affected IDs, history inverse and which 2D/3D, optical, simulation or manufacturing caches need regeneration. Selection tools may return a typed read-only selection result; they must not report an edit revision unless source data changed.

Present evidence / honest capability state

Observed registry mode: native. Native step changes simulated cloth state; pause stops scheduled steps. This describes the Rust V2 implementation snapshot, not a guarantee that an analogous browser V1 tool is absent. A complete implementation needs a real native GUI and service exercise and must not inherit success from a placeholder control.

Acceptance workflow

Click with valid state, run without selection, undo/restart and assert no fake execution or mislabeled support. Also verify keyboard/flyout focus, tooltip truth, no-op handling, cancellation, deterministic error messages and visible selection feedback at 390px viewport.

ScenarioPass criterion
NominalNative tool executes its named action on a valid target; expected geometry/property delta confirmed
Undo/reopenUndo restores full source state; redo and restart reconstruct identical entity IDs and topology
Bad selectionNo silent wrong-object mutation; clear disabled/error reason; source revision does not change
2D ↔ 3DChanges highlight and transfer to paired source/mesh, or capability accurately marked read-only

3D · B · B053D editing / avatarUNAVAILABLEnot yet routed

Observed purpose and target user action

Edit 3D Pen (Avatar) is a separately named control in the 3D reference viewport. Exact interaction contracts, flyout variants and selection preconditions must be confirmed against the saved CLO 7.0.242 reference session. In EXO, selection must resolve to the expected authored object kind for 3d editing / avatar; an active icon or cursor alone is insufficient.

Required native implementation

Map hit-tested 3D triangle and barycentric coordinates to source pattern, update surface or arrangement state, retain pose and stable ID lineage under remeshing.

Data and transaction contract

SurfaceAttachment, Pose and GarmentFrame. Use stable object identifiers, physical millimeter/centimeter units where geometry is involved, explicit typed parameters and expectedRevision. The command must return affected IDs, history inverse and which 2D/3D, optical, simulation or manufacturing caches need regeneration. Selection tools may return a typed read-only selection result; they must not report an edit revision unless source data changed.

Present evidence / honest capability state

Observed registry mode: unavailable. Needs persistent 3D projected pen/stroke geometry with editable anchors, surface attachment, undo and export. Existing 2D construction lines are not a substitute. This describes the Rust V2 implementation snapshot, not a guarantee that an analogous browser V1 tool is absent. A complete implementation needs a real native GUI and service exercise and must not inherit success from a placeholder control.

Acceptance workflow

Orbit, pick near an occluded edge, edit in native 3D, inspect 2D mapping, undo and reopen across a remesh. Also verify keyboard/flyout focus, tooltip truth, no-op handling, cancellation, deterministic error messages and visible selection feedback at 390px viewport.

ScenarioPass criterion
NominalNative tool executes its named action on a valid target; expected geometry/property delta confirmed
Undo/reopenUndo restores full source state; redo and restart reconstruct identical entity IDs and topology
Bad selectionNo silent wrong-object mutation; clear disabled/error reason; source revision does not change
2D ↔ 3DChanges highlight and transfer to paired source/mesh, or capability accurately marked read-only

The command must return affected IDs, history inverse and which 2D/3D, optical, simulation or manufacturing caches need regeneration. Selection tools may return a typed read-only selection result; they must not report an edit revision unless source data changed.

Present evidence / honest capability state

This describes the Rust V2 implementation snapshot, not a guarantee that an analogous browser V1 tool is absent. A complete implementation needs a real native GUI and service exercise and must not inherit success from a placeholder control.

Acceptance workflow

VERIFICATION QA-01

Execution matrix and hard numerical release gates

A completed feature proves named behavior through more than a compile or screenshot. For every tool: dispatch, preconditions, model mutation, history, reopen, 2D/3D mapping, alternate runtime behavior and negative fixtures. For each fabric: stable ID, swatch, units, optical file hash, solver assignment and actual calibration state. For each manufacturer export: independent CAD consumption and dimensional verification.

GateMandatory evidenceFailure prevents
Source hygieneClean source clone, locked dependencies, no private external assetsrelease build
Native bridgeSigned application launch, command response, revision persistencenative-ready toolbar label
GeometryUnit and tolerance report on curved boundaries, darts, seam allowancesCAD-complete claim
SolverWhole-trajectory contact, seam, strain and drift metricsfit accepted status
MaterialsSpecimen readings, optical map sources, unit mappingmeasured/calibrated badge
ManufacturingThird-party DXF roundtrip, factory sample sign-offfactory-approved release
SecurityLocal service scoping, import validation, rights isolationcommunity publication

When a fixture fails, preserve its revision and numeric maximum error, avoid replacing the baseline screenshot and record the exact test binary paths. Historical 58/58 native CTest claims are not the same as a freshly configured build; reconfigure out of tree where stale CMake caches refer to old checkout paths.

REFERENCE INDEX QA-02

Official CLO research and compatibility boundaries

The following links are public documented behavior to guide independent EXO workflows. CLO’s current web articles describe multiple newer 2026 features, while the live observed toolbar positions are from a pinned installed 7.0.242 session. Keep these as two separate baseline versions. EXO implementation should use original assets, original code and licensed media.

ResourceEngineering reason
CLO live 7.0.242 inventoryProject live app inspector; four columns of 20+18+17+15 controls.
CLO physical fabrication documentationFabric emulator measures directional physical sample responses; not automatically EXO data.
CLO material settingsFront/Back/Side optical property model and PBR settings.
CLO DXF interoperabilityIndustrial AAMA/ASTM/standard DXF import/export options and losses to test.
CLO gradingSize groups, rules and grade editing behaviors.
CLO pattern informationPattern metadata, revision and shrinkage requirements.
CLO particle distanceNumerical cloth mesh resolution property in mm.
CLO sewingSewing selection/mutation behavior.
CLO POMPermanent measurement geometry editing behavior.
CLO finishingOptical map generation options.
CLO 2D seamsCustom seam allowance editing and selection.
CLO recent grading review2026 grading side-by-side review; future parity scope.
Licensed optical scan registryCC0 third-party scan record and checksums.

Do not infer CLO internal solver constants, unpublished Photoshop-like wash masks, 400 exclusive CLO shipped fabrics, or proprietary project file completeness from these pages. Vendor descriptions show reference user behavior, not an authorization to copy intellectual property.

Reference URLs are enumerated in the accompanying Markdown source and HTML. For audit, fetch a dated copy or retain article identification and update dates; mark unconfirmed behavior explicitly. The design goal is comparable usefulness with original EXO implementation.

SOURCE ANNEX 01 · DATED EXISTING RESEARCH

GARMENT_STUDIO_COMPLETE_CLO3D_PARITY_MASTER_PLAN_2026-10-13.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

Garment Studio — exhaustive CLO-class master engineering plan and checklist

Created 2026-10-13. Scope: entire application, 2D CAD, 3D tools, fitting/simulation, native app, manufacture, automation and QA. This is a living inventory, not proof of CLO3D feature parity or a claim of having checked every feature of every CLO release.

> Safety of current work: This roadmap is documentation only. Existing uncommitted source edits, garments, simulation fixtures, personal avatars and local builds were not reset, replaced or deleted.

1. Executive interpretation, status and priority vocabulary

  • Baseline: prior user-facing estimate ~30–35% feature parity was rough opinion, not a measured benchmark; do not convert into a burndown percentage without actual scored acceptance data.
  • Current source evidence: React/Vite + Three.js UI (src/App.jsx, src/components/*); core CAD/production modules (src/core/*); JS PBD, C++ native XPBD and Metal/PPF bridge; Blender bridge; native macOS bundle; many engineering check commands in package.json.
  • Verified historically: a production build, the consolidated non-simulation engineering checks, and a signed/opened native bundle in an earlier pass. No new builds/tests are claimed by this planning document.
  • Known important defects (from previous audits): floating garment and unacceptable strain; cloth-body and cloth-self intersections in dynamic fitting; incomplete CAD constraints and styleline operations; incomplete industrial interchange; shallow hierarchy/editor actions; UI automation selector ambiguity.
  • P0 = correctness or foundation blocker; P1 = significant workflow parity; P2 = production polish / advanced breadth. All unchecked boxes below are *work/verification required*, not a declaration that the code does not yet exist. A box can be marked done only with direct evidence linked to a test and artifact.

1A. Traceability rules and honest scope boundaries

Each GS entry is an atomic capability criterion, not yet a resolved task ticket. To execute it, split into independently estimated tickets for design, implementation, unit tests, integration tests, native acceptance and docs. Before claiming parity, compare against a specified CLO3D product version, platform and documented command behavior using original assets. The expression “every possible feature” is open-ended; this plan aims for broad engineering coverage and must grow as verified gaps are discovered.

Technical-debt and defect baseline: preserve all uncommitted local changes; do not infer absence from an unsearched module; quantify solver regressions only under the same baseline; treat disabled/placeholder controls as incomplete but avoid calling a test timeout a product defect. Each backlog entry stays open until evidence shows it passes the universal acceptance gates. A source file existing is not acceptance evidence.

2. Universal Definition of Done for every feature

For every feature below, the assignee must complete all of these gates before marking it done:

  • [ ] G-01 User workflow and precision requirements written down.
  • [ ] G-02 Existing implementation inspected; duplicate capability avoided.
  • [ ] G-03 Data model and units defined; stable IDs/provenance preserved.
  • [ ] G-04 Behavior works with real project documents, not a UI-only mock.
  • [ ] G-05 Undo and redo both restore exact state.
  • [ ] G-06 Save, close, reopen preserves feature state and required assets.
  • [ ] G-07 2D↔3D linkage is correct where applicable.
  • [ ] G-08 Import/export and manufacturing consequences tested where applicable.
  • [ ] G-09 Numeric edge cases, units, precision, degenerate geometry tested.
  • [ ] G-10 Native Mac and browser/preview interaction checked where applicable.
  • [ ] G-11 Automated regression plus representative manual quality review.
  • [ ] G-12 Performance, cancellation and error feedback checked.
  • [ ] G-13 No regression in other apparel samples and avatar poses.
  • [ ] G-14 Docs, feature matrix, provenance and acceptance evidence updated.

3. Detailed system-by-system backlog

Checkbox IDs are permanent roadmap references. Use them as issue IDs in future agent passes. Each line is a discrete testable target, sometimes requiring several engineering subtasks. Long-term acceptance must be grounded in verified samples and not just a screenshot.

4. Confirmed / recorded failures and diagnostic worklist

These are not speculative parity wishes; they come from the existing engineering audit and need current-build reproduction before a bug is closed.

  • [ ] BUG-001 Floating shirt: reproduce body-to-neck/shoulder clearance and collar lift with current Thomas geometry; diagnose competing anchors and collider overlap; fix; verify all frames.
  • [ ] BUG-002 Excess strain: prior 180 cm tee scenario recorded peak ~546% relative extension; reproduce with version/preset, locate source constraints, reduce to measured material tolerances, not merely a permissive regression cap.
  • [ ] BUG-003 Dynamic intersections: prior 10-frame PPF tee recorded final 177 avatar intersection pairs and 56 self-intersection pairs; retest; implement CCD/coupled contact and certify continuous trajectory.
  • [ ] BUG-004 Sewing/contact conflict: quantify each solver pass residual and resulting displacement; remove seam reopenings and interpenetrations.
  • [ ] BUG-005 PPF long-sew timeout: profile pathological contact graph; enforce cancellability and convergence thresholds, valid failure reporting.
  • [ ] BUG-006 Fixture identity: ensure 2D source pattern, 3D pose, avatar scale and collider mesh have exactly matching transform/units and source IDs across backends.
  • [ ] BUG-007 UI QA selector ambiguity: prior scripts/qa-clo-interface.mjs matched multiple “Free sewing” buttons. Replace brittle exact-name assumptions with semantic selectors; re-run native/browser E2E.
  • [ ] BUG-008 Grading test coverage: prior audit found missing scripts/check-grading.mjs command; add explicit grading golden tests, then register in consolidated QA.
  • [ ] BUG-009 Mesh/styleline partial tooling: audit currently disabled/planned controls and incomplete mesh selection styles; verify actual state after latest patches; implement true persistent operations with 2D mapping.
  • [ ] BUG-010 DXF fidelity beyond existing golden samples: verify rotated buttonholes, grainlines, notches, internal lines, curves, nested graded pieces, scaling and industrial vendor interchange.
  • [ ] BUG-011 Browser visual QA timeout under concurrent builds: isolate stable dedicated QA run and save success/failure artifacts rather than counting timeout as pass.
  • [ ] BUG-012 Multiple-actor edit safety: document ownership, avoid resets/cleans and fixture overwrites; snapshot before destructive migrations; verify simultaneous native build processes do not race.

5. Milestones and dependency gates

MilestoneGoal and deliverablesBlocking gatesEvidence required
M0: Document integrityCanonical schema, action API, migrations, undo/save and source mappingNoneOpen/modify/save/reopen/undo/redo across multiple fixtures
M1: Manufacturer-ready 2DExact CAD primitives/constraints, seam allowances, T-shirt/jeans base patternsM0Golden numeric patterns, fixture import/export tolerances
M2: Seam graph & meshingDurable edge IDs, seam orientation, adaptive mesh, complete flat-to-3D mappingM0–M1Closed sewing graph and persistent remesh with stable mappings
M3: Physical fit truthUnified avatar skin collider, CCD, coupled seam/contact, calibrated materialsM1–M2Zero unsafe crossings across trajectory, acceptable fabric strain/gaps
M4: Reliable auto-fitInferred arrangement, reversible tweaks and scored candidate acceptanceM3Tee fits at 160/180/200 cm across multiple poses
M5: Professional editing3D selection/stylelines, hierarchical browser, property editing, trims and fabricsM0–M2Full multi-tool E2E CRUD + synchronized 2D/3D editing
M6: Factory outputsGrading, BOM, POM, markers, standards-grounded exportM1,M5Vendor fixture cross-import and production package completeness
M7: Complete visualsAvatar poses, rendering, animation, library and Blender interchangeM3,M5Consistent assets and approved rendered scenes
M8: Release qualityNative packaging, full regression, performance, security, docsAll previousRelease checklist, benchmark matrix, migration and recovery drills

6. Focused next-pass implementation order

  1. Do not lose existing source: inspect working tree, confirm local backups and running solver processes; note other agents editing files.
  2. Fix structural reliability first: canonical schema, piece/edge/seam IDs, explicit unit conversion, command transactions and undo/save tests.
  3. Complete the precision drafting golden shirt: true four-piece pattern, seam graph, measured allowances, tolerance and grading fixtures.
  4. Add full-trajectory physical quality gates before more visual tweaks: per-frame body/self contact, strain, seam p95/max and settling using the exact posed Thomas collider.
  5. Couple solver contact, stitching and material behavior: maintain correct arrangement; never accept a fit only because a render looks good.
  6. Close 3D/editor workflow gaps: editable stylelines and mesh selection, hierarchical Object Browser and numeric inspector, all reversible.
  7. Industrial export and calibration: validate apparel factory files on external consumers and versioned benchmark fixtures.
  8. **Advance rendering, animation and asset library only after verified data integrity and fitting milestones are stable.

7. Ownership and reporting template

For each engineering pass record: ID | current evidence | exact files | fixture | before metrics | implementation | after metrics | automated test output | manual screenshot | native app build | status | next blocker. Statuses: unreviewed, confirmed absent, partial, implemented-unverified, verified, blocked. Do not promote partial to verified on build-pass alone.

SOURCE ANNEX 02 · DATED EXISTING RESEARCH

EXO_MASTER_ROADMAP.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

EXO Sample 3D — engineering master roadmap

Status: proposed execution plan, 15 October 2026. Product identity: EXO Sample 3D. Repository: mikristian/exo-sample-3d; historical document filenames and internal identifiers are preserved for traceability. This roadmap is a contract for work and verification, not a declaration that the application matches CLO3D or produces certified factory deliverables.

Product contract and architectural boundaries

EXO aims to connect precise editable 2D patterns, sewn 3D geometry, realistic fabric responses, fit analysis, visual material design, and traceable manufacturing packages. A single versioned garment project must remain the source of truth for all views. An edit to a dart, seam allowance or grade rule should invalidate the relevant derived mesh, contact state, point-of-measure (POM) results, marker and export outputs. The application should never display an old simulation or export as if it reflected newer drafting geometry.

The source currently includes a React/Three.js browser workspace, a shipped macOS shell using WebKit, native C++ feature modules under native_cpp/core/, a developing Qt interface, and an optional Rust PPF sidecar. The active UI and the tested native CLI do not yet constitute a single independently validated fully native application. Existing C++ parity coverage is module-specific. JavaScript remains a reference behavior for many functions, and a close numerical match to JavaScript is not proof that the reference physics is physically correct. Preserve that distinction in status and release notes.

Read [architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md), [native conversion checkpoint](SAMPL3D_NATIVE_CPP_CONVERSION_LOOP_2026-10-15.md), [CLO production comparison](CLO_EXPORT_PRODUCTION_PARITY_2026-10-15.md), [simulation research](EXO_SIMULATION_RESEARCH.md) and [verification plan](EXO_VALIDATION_TEST_PLAN.md) together. The October production audit explicitly states that 90% CLO3D parity has not been demonstrated. Neither the existence of export files nor passing source parity tests is equivalent to acceptance by a cutter, grader, vendor or garment technician.

Work graph and dependency gates

The milestones below are numbered by dependency and verification effort, not by an asserted percentage complete. Parallel tasks are safe where they operate on separate data contracts and cannot silently alter shared geometry. Every GitHub milestone requires a named owner, evidence artifact, exact command, machine/OS/solver version, failure case, and acceptance reviewer. Do not promote an issue solely because a feature appears in a menu.

M0 — reproducibility, provenance and reference corpus
  1. Capture immutable source revision, build flags, native runtime versions, material configuration and test seed with each fixture. Define schema revisions for garment projects, physics snapshots, export manifests and acceptance records. Store fixture metadata beside the fixture and avoid environment-dependent absolute paths.
  2. Establish open, redistributable base garments: basic tee with conventional sleeves, sleeveless shell, trousers with inseam and fly, jacket with zipper and multi-layer cloth. Include explicit unit scales, grain angles, notches, panel winding, seams, symmetric references and annotated measurement landmarks. Use synthetic or freely licensed avatar geometries with documented redistribution rights.
  3. Add a deterministic cross-backend harness that compares reference JavaScript outputs, native C++ transformations and Rust solver traces by operation and field. Declare tolerances per field and explain why they are physically meaningful. Every mismatch retains a minimal repro and observed numeric delta.
  4. Set protected CI checks for format validity, build reproducibility, license inventory, data migrations, and sample exports. Run npm run build, npm run check:non-simulation, scripts/check-native-cpp-conversion-coverage.mjs and native test suites as applicable. The inventory is a set of covered exports, not a whole-product percentage.

Exit: on a clean checkout, another developer can run a documented subset without private assets; fixture licenses are declared; every baseline passes or its failures are visible. See [open-source validation](OPEN_SOURCE_VALIDATION_2026-10-14.md), [release audit](OPEN_SOURCE_RELEASE_READINESS_AUDIT_2026-10-13.md), and [licensing](LICENSING.md).

M1 — CAD accuracy and round-trip drafting

Keep all native drafting coordinates in declared physical units. Design a stable document model for panels, vertices, quadratic/curved edges, seam allowances, darts, internal lines, cutouts, grainlines, labels, linked mirror transforms, user-defined construction points and per-size grade rules. Editing must preserve constraint identity and undo/redo history. A notch tied to an edge parameter should stay attached after reasonable edits and should become a validation error if its parent edge is deleted. Imported patterns must be quarantined from simulation until winding, unit scale and topological checks pass.

Validate exact millimeter bounding geometry, edge sampling error, seam length, reversible transformations, nested undo groups, dangling seam references, nonmanifold contours and self-intersections. Cover keyboard and pointer tools, snapping in actual 2D viewport coordinates, scale/zoom behavior, high-DPI selection and redraw responsiveness. Existing relevant files include src/core/dxf.js, src/core/grading.js, src/core/measurements.js, and native tests such as scripts/check-native-cpp-drafting-constructions.mjs, scripts/check-native-cpp-cad.mjs and scripts/check-native-cpp-project-geometry.mjs. Read [2D drafting acceptance](CLO_DRAFTING_WORKSPACE_MATERIAL_ACCEPTANCE_2026-10-14.md) before changing interaction contracts.

Exit: reference patterns survive save/open and export/import without lost construction semantics, and tolerances have been verified against independent geometry assertions. Persisted projects remain readable following schema upgrades.

M2 — sewn garment topology and mesh generation

Map seam segments to discretized mesh boundary edges using stable parent edge identifiers and arc-length parameters. Support opposing directions, seam-length imbalances, easing, pleats, darts, virtual zipper tracks, reversible seam assignments, layered seam junctions and incremental remeshing. Report sewable lengths, unmatched intervals and topology violations before assembling. A sewing error must remain visible even if a visual interpolation can hide the opening in a posed screenshot.

Generate UVs in grain-relative coordinates, preserve panel names/materials after subdivision, and propagate fabric orientation into anisotropic constitutive models. Verify triangle area and orientation, boundary invariants, panel spacing, and seam matching under changes to tessellation density. native_cpp/core/pattern_mesh.hpp, scripts/check-native-cpp-pattern-mesh.mjs, scripts/check-native-cpp-seam-assembly-parity-bridge.mjs and [native mesh audit](NATIVE_MESH_QUALITY_PASS_2026-10-13.md) provide starting evidence. The advanced assembly tests must report contact and position agreement across tee and trousers rather than converting a one-frame pass into a complete fit claim.

Exit: deterministic, editable sewn rest state across multiple garment classes, with explicitly tracked invalid or incomplete seams.

M3 — physically defensible dynamics and fitting

Maintain a clear separation between real-time design preview and a slower acceptance-grade simulation. The browser solver may support immediate iteration; the Rust PPF contact solver may own physically demanding contact operations; C++ may own CAD, interchange, quality analysis and glue. A single orchestrator must expose backend identity, integration scheme, timestep, substeps, cloth thickness, material parameter units, initial pose, collision proxy source and solver stopping criteria. The viewport must not collapse diagnostics into an unexplained green status.

Implement and verify stretch, shear, bending, damping, mass, gravity, seams and boundary pins. Treat self-contact and continuous collision detection (CCD) as distinct requirements from body penetration correction. Tests must cover fast arm motion, cloth-on-cloth folds, crossing sleeve panels, layered jackets, narrow armholes, seam convergence and warm-start resets. Evaluate swept cloth/body and cloth/cloth interactions through complete trajectories. Require controlled comparisons against licensed or open physical benchmark data and physically measured drapes. See [simulation research](EXO_SIMULATION_RESEARCH.md), [PPF body tee pipeline](PPF_BODY_TEE_PIPELINE.md), [contact safety](PPF_CONTACT_SAFETY_NEXT_PASS_2026-10-13.md) and [actual fit audit](PPF_ACTUAL_GARMENT_FIT_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-10-14.md).

Exit: recurring simulation fixtures meet numerical, geometric and trajectory acceptance limits recorded in the [test plan](EXO_VALIDATION_TEST_PLAN.md). Until then, any status marked fitCertified, factoryCertified or CCD-complete remains false unless independently justified by the exact scenario.

M4 — fabric appearance and engineering parameters

Retain separate appearance and mechanical material models. Procedural albedo, normal, height and roughness do not establish bending stiffness or friction. Keep physical provenance for warp/weft elasticity, bending, density, damping, thickness and surface friction, with confidence intervals where measurements are uncertain. Support both isotropic and anisotropic profiles, grain rotation, different materials per panel, trims and hardware collision boundaries. Map UI parameters to defined units and solver-specific constitutive fields.

The current project describes 23 fabric presets and 13 procedural textile structures; inspect scripts/check-native-cpp-textile-procedurals.mjs, scripts/check-native-cpp-fabric-pbr-pixels.mjs and [fabric parity audit](SAMPL3D_FABRIC_PARITY_AUDIT_2026-10-15.md). Validate continuity at UV seams, deterministic generated maps, texture-scale consistency in centimeters and visual behavior under changing lighting. Changing appearance should not accidentally restart drape; changing mechanical parameters should invalidate physics caches and visibly request a new solve.

Exit: appearance reproducibility plus measured or explicitly estimated physical parameters, accompanied by uncluttered material provenance indicators.

M5 — fit analysis, avatars and animation

Formalize avatar import, mesh hygiene, collision normals, body landmarks, pose binding, shape scaling and motion interpolation. Create signed-distance or mesh contact proxies with documented error bounds and a way to compare proxy results with source triangles. Separate cloth strain from pressure estimates, local clearance from penetration depth, and posture feasibility from garment size recommendations. A color-coded strain map needs a legend, normalization rule and per-material thresholds. Expose analysis quality flags when motion sampling or contact convergence is inadequate.

Run baseline and perturbation tests on the tee, trousers and layered outfit, multiple sizes, and full motion sequences. Do not ship private scan assets or embedded proprietary avatars. Use open synthetic human meshes with correct notices. If a real reference body is used locally for testing, publish anonymized derived metrics only where distribution and privacy permissions permit.

Exit: trustworthy measurement landmarks, explainable heat maps and a repeatable on-body acceptance demonstration including failure cases.

M6 — manufacturing handoff

Use the [production pipeline](EXO_PRODUCTION_PIPELINE.md) as the exporter contract. Verify industrial 2D DXF interpretations, unit scaling, grade-rule preservation, marker orientation, lengths, material widths, physical plot scale, POM tolerances, and a complete BOM. Keep revision identifiers consistent across source project, drawings, grades, cut orders, costing and ZIP manifest. Test a complete handoff with a cooperating technician and at least one separate CAD/cutter system.

Current work already covers several source and native operations: see [CLO export comparison](CLO_EXPORT_PRODUCTION_PARITY_2026-10-15.md), scripts/check-production-handoff.mjs, scripts/check-factory.mjs, scripts/check-native-cpp-factory-cli.mjs, scripts/check-native-cpp-markers.mjs, and [native BOM/marker audit](NATIVE_CPP_BOM_AND_MARKER_PARITY_2026-10-15.md). Treat byte parity, unit parity and manufacturer compatibility as three different forms of evidence.

Exit: authorized pilot cutting from controlled reference patterns with signed dimensional QC. Factory certification requires documented supplier approval and an actual physical production review.

M7 — desktop consolidation and open-source release

Once contracts above stabilize, offer an independently built C++/Qt desktop with equivalent authoring, undo, 2D/3D navigation, project save/load and production actions. The user can continue working in the existing WebKit workspace until verified workflows move. Rust PPF can remain a separate process exposed over a stable versioned protocol; a Rust solver does not force a Rust UI. Confirm Qt distribution terms and upstream binary/source licenses before packaging. A public repository should include legitimate screenshots, reproducible demo assets and the V16 promo only if all media rights and asset licenses permit redistribution.

Exit: reproducible packaging, clean-machine smoke test, explicit bundled licenses, public issue board, vulnerability handling and reviewed release notes. See [Qt toolchain](CPP_QT6_TOOLCHAIN_READINESS_2026-10-15.md), [native roadmap](GARMENT_STUDIO_NATIVE_ROADMAP.md), [GitHub launch](OPEN_SOURCE_GITHUB_LAUNCH_PLAYBOOK.md) and [contribution guide](EXO_CONTRIBUTION_GUIDE.md).

Issue board taxonomy and prioritization

Create labels cad, sewing, mesh, physics, ppf-rust, contact, material, avatar, fit, factory, interchange, desktop, docs, license, verification, plus severity labels. An issue's first paragraph defines the user-visible failure or missing workflow; subsequent fields specify input fixture, expected behavior, observed behavior, candidate modules, acceptance gate, regression command and proof attachment. An umbrella tracking issue may link implementation, independent verification and external pilot tasks, but each subissue must close on its own artifact.

Prioritize defects that can misrepresent a garment's physical viability: silently inverted normals, body penetration, stale simulation shown after editing, wrong DXF units, altered grade dimensions, and false factory-ready flags. Next come fidelity improvements that influence purchase or manufacturing decisions; then rendering aesthetics, workspace refinement and optional interchange formats. A striking promo shot never substitutes for evidence of a solved collision or a measured cut dimension.

Decision record and release rule

For every migration, record a brief architecture decision: the data ownership change, why C++/Rust/JavaScript is appropriate, serialization boundary, numerical agreement conditions, backward compatibility, rollback route and test provenance. Preserve a testable reference path until coverage is sufficient to remove it. Avoid hardcoded fixture-specific output, test-only flags hidden from users or declaring coverage using only counted exports. A release can label modules experimental, fixture-verified, pilot-validated or production-approved only with attached proof of that particular claim. Maintain a visible unsupported-functions matrix rather than compressing the roadmap into a fabricated completion score.

Garment Studio vs CLO3D: whole-application gap audit (excluding cloth simulation)

Date: 2026-10-13. Scope: repository src/, native_app/, scripts/, docs/, production/export modules. Excludes solving/draping/cloth-body contact quality. This is a source/feature and bounded automated-test audit, not a certified parity review of every CLO version or an exhaustive interactive end-to-end test.

Evidence and test results

  • npm run build: PASS.
  • node scripts/check-production.mjs: PASS.
  • node scripts/check-factory.mjs: PASS.
  • node scripts/qa-clo-interface.mjs: FAIL, selector ambiguity (getByRole('button',{name:'Free sewing'}) now matches two buttons). This is a UI test reliability issue, not proof that sewing is broken.
  • node scripts/check-grading.mjs: unavailable / missing script; grading has an implementation in src/core/grading.js, but that test command did not verify it.
  • Existing detailed implementation and inventory reports: docs/CLO_UI_IMPLEMENTATION_2026_10_13.md, docs/CLO_UI_TOOL_INVENTORY.md, docs/CLO_ICON_VISUAL_AUDIT_2026-10-13.md, docs/CLO_DUAL_COLUMN_TOOLBAR_PLACEMENT.md.

Status terms: Foundation = usable core with substantial missing workflows; Partial = limited subset or UI-to-inspector only; Absent/unverified = no validated complete implementation found in inspected code. None means CLO parity.

Recommended order (outside simulation)

  1. P0 CAD correctness: establish editable topology, exact numeric geometry, reliable undo/redo, selection, snap and DXF round-trip. This is the foundation for every downstream feature.
  2. P0 real 3D tool implementation: replace disabled mesh selection and styleline controls with real operations that propagate to 2D and persist.
  3. P1 editing completeness: sewing graph editor, trims/topstitch/puckering, Object Browser and Property Editor semantics.
  4. P1 production reliability: grading, marker, tech packs and DXF import/export acceptance fixtures; do not equate existence of a generator with factory-level certification.
  5. P1 automation and QA: expose safe, reversible typed operations for AI and users; test every toolbar command against model mutation, undo, save/load and 2D/3D synchronization.
  6. P2 presentation ecosystem: material library, avatar authoring, high-quality rendering, animation, asset management and customizable workspace.

Verification contract

For each CLO comparison tool, log implemented, partial, planned or unsupported, link source function and screenshot, then test: click -> intended model mutation -> undo -> redo -> save/load -> 2D/3D sync -> DXF/production export if applicable. Include numerical geometric checks, not only browser screenshot assertions. CLO version and toolbar personalization must be recorded for any 1:1 comparison. Do not silently activate placeholder buttons or imply a full feature from a tab or menu label.

SOURCE ANNEX 04 · DATED EXISTING RESEARCH

CLO3D_PROPERTY_EDITOR_DEEP_AUDIT_20261016.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

CLO Property Editor deep audit and EXO V2 parity specification

Date: 2026-10-16. Deliverable: official-reference inventory, local evidence boundaries, EXO V2 mapping, priority and acceptance criteria. Scope: right-side selection-sensitive Property Editor, including linked Object Browser property contexts. This is a design and source audit; it is not a claim that the installed CLO application's rendered UI was visually captured.

Evidence, version boundaries, and how to read this audit

  • O means the control or behavior appears in an official CLO Help Center article. The linked pages are living documents and can describe newer releases than CLO 7.0.242. Therefore O does not automatically mean verified in 7.0.242.
  • L? means exact label, order, defaults, ranges, visibility, and behavior must be checked in the *locally running* CLO 7.0.242 Property Editor. No screenshots or interaction with that application were available during this worker audit. Existing project audit records identify /Applications/CLO_Standalone_OnlineAuth.app, but a bundle's presence is not evidence of an active 7.0.242 session.
  • E means directly supported by the current EXO V2 source examined on October 16, particularly src/components/CloPropertyEditorAccordion.jsx. E-partial means UI or data exists without verified matching physical semantics; E-no means no matching editor/solver binding was established in that inspected code. Other project work may be landing concurrently; verify after integration.
  • P means proposed future EXO behavior. Do not present P or L? as an observed CLO screenshot fact.

Primary official reference: Property Editor. This confirms that the editor changes according to selected pattern, internal shape, sewing, stitch, sewing style, fabric, button, buttonhole and avatar, with simulation/wind/2D-window properties invoked via background context menus. Numeric fields support right-click Calculator and Convert, and items can carry an Item No. The specific properties below must be dispatched by selection object kind, not merely the active toolbar tab.

Important version caution: The Help Center includes older article families and pages updated in 2025–2026. For example Select Binding explicitly calls out a *Both (ver 7.0)* placement option, while newer seam-tape articles refer to 2024.2 migration. Record the local build and screenshot before calling an individual feature “exact CLO 7.0.242 parity.”

Current EXO V2 code facts (read-only October 16 snapshot)

The editor at src/components/CloPropertyEditorAccordion.jsx exports eight IDs: information, selected-line, simulation, fabric, bond, solidify, arrange, and miscellaneous. A compact Property Editor remains docked below the Object Browser, with expandable sections. It distinguishes a Rust selection.kind === 'line' from pattern, draft line selection, some Object Browser kinds, and project fallback, but does not yet have dedicated property forms for every CLO object kind.

On a selected Rust line the editor displays 2D Line Length, 3D Line Length, 3D Stretch, sewing-link IDs, and an editable Target 2D length (cm) specification (0.01–1000 cm, only with a Rust source and setter). Source text correctly states that a target length specification is *not* an elastic force. Draft-only selection can provide a 2D length while leaving 3D length unavailable. For Rust patterns, measured 2D/3D perimeters are displayed. Elastic and Seam Taping have disabled controls and explanatory readouts; Bond and Solidify are explicitly unconfigured. The simulation panel can issue simulate/pause/reset callbacks. Fabric panel displays assigned fabric and, in draft mode, swatch/GSM. Arrangement can report position X/Y/Z or draft staging offsets, but these do not establish CLO arrangement-point semantics. The rendering of children under the active workspace and the pattern-properties portal should remain intact during future upgrades.

EXO evidence pointers: CloPropertyEditorAccordion.jsx:8-27 group mapping; :38-50 selection/type resolution; :85-110 measured line and unavailable forces; :113-160 simulation, fabric, Bond, Solidify, staging and portal; :166-185 accordion shell. See also docs/CLO3D_PROPERTY_EDITOR_AUDIT_20261016.md and docs/EXO_V2_RIGHT_PROPERTY_EDITOR_20261016.md. These documents reflect earlier checkpoints; when they disagree about V2 measured-line support, the current source governs.

Field notation

In each matrix, R = read-only computed/display, N = number, B = boolean, S = dropdown/enum, T = text, C = color/material picker, A = action. A range/default marked ? is not confirmed by the cited source and requires a 7.0.242 capture. Length defaults follow project unit settings (mm/cm/in) when applicable; do not hardwire centimeters as CLO's universal display unit. -- is unknown/unavailable, not zero.

Legacy article lists slightly different particle-distance range; use version capture.
**Fabric asset**: Object Browser FabricName (T), physical-property **Preset** (S) and Detail (N fields), assigned usages (R)[Property Editor](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115004877468-Property-Editor); [CLO designers on changing physical preset](https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/community/posts/4404012517657-Issue-of-puffiness)E-partial: swatch/name/GSM, native fabric library linked through workspaceSeparate a selected **fabric asset** from a selected **pattern wearing fabric**. Saving should retain physical preset and axes.

Exact CLO labels L?.

Conditional behavior and exactness rules

  1. Selection drives schema. pattern, outerEdge, internalLine, internalShape, sewingPair, seamTape, topstitchStyle, topstitchInstance, binding, fabricAsset, graphic, button, buttonhole, avatar, gradedPiece, POM, arrangementPoint, simulationSettings, windSettings, window2D, or none. The current generalized eight-group accordion can stay as shell, but only context-valid sections may appear. No stale pattern name/measurement after deselection.
  2. Every numeric field declares dimensionality: length (mm/cm/in), area, ratio %, angle degrees, SPI, thread thickness unit (Tex/Tkt/Nm/Den/mm), per-area mass (GSM), or dimensionless scalar. UI conversions never rewrite physical meaning. Null/unmapped values are Unavailable.
  3. Do not invent defaults. Documented values are Elastic Strength 10, Elastic Ratio 100%, and ratio range 0–200%; Skive 1–100%; topstitch max five layers; Binding Width 1–50 mm, Length 50–200%, Particle Distance 4–20 mm as described in the linked current binding article; Fold Angle 0–360°. The remainder are L? until version-specific capture.
  4. CLO instance versus asset: a Topstitch or Fabric style entry has shared specifications; its instance on a garment has spatial placement/assignment. Editing the selected garment line must not silently change the shared global style.
  5. Conditional disabling: topstitch Flip inactive on pattern cutting lines; stitch-line distance only for multiple lines; stitch OBJ optimization only in OBJ mode; binding Extend must have valid surrounding geometry; sewing Fold fields depend on Turned state; pattern arrangement-point fields require an arrangement point; native physics toggles require a solver-capability response.
  6. Transactions: mutation includes selected object ID, revision and validated units, and must reject stale updates or missing geometry. Document model update, render update, undo/redo and saved reload must agree.
  7. Multi-select: heterogeneous contexts show common compatible fields and mixed values; homogeneous edges may expose Total Length. Never silently choose the first target.
  8. Version comparability: capture CLO 7.0.242 (exact About dialog/build) with selected pattern, outer edge, internal line/shape, sewing line, taped segment, topstitch asset/instance, Fabric asset, graphic, button/hole, avatar, graded piece, POM, Bond/Skive, arrangement point, and blank 2D/3D canvas. Capture expanded accordions, units, tool, disabled states, tooltip, and one benign field change per context. Record screenshot names and observations in a follow-on evidence appendix.

Prioritized implementation roadmap

PriorityConcrete deliverableAcceptance gate
**P0-A**Add typed selection dispatch and per-object capability model across 2D, 3D, Object Browser. Keep existing eight accordion visual shell and hosted advanced property controls working.Clicking each available object shows its identity and correct context; gray background clears selection; no stale values; header and visible groups stable.
**P0-B**Complete trustworthy measured geometry: individual outer edges, entire pattern perimeters, source 2D UV vs current mapped simulated 3D path, group totals, units. Preserve current Rust line measurement implementation.Known 10 cm straight edge reads 10 cm 2D; pulled geometry can read >10 cm 3D; unmapped line returns --; change cm to mm/in without mutating stored length.
**P0-C**Central field registry with `kind,type,unit,min,max,step,default,readOnly,availability,tooltip,command` and source-specific verification.Unknown/range-invalid numbers rejected; disabled fields cannot dispatch; numeric calculator converts dimensions accurately.
**P1-A**Outer/internal Elastic and Seam Tape schemas, native edge constraints and reinforcement material transfer to Rust; separate sewing-pair tension/fold commands.Solver parameter actually updates; monitored force/rest target changes and simulated cloth behavior responds. UI refuses unsupported source/selection.
**P1-B**Fabric asset physics and visual appearance inspector, with material calibration provenance; related style/instance editors for topstitch, graphics, button/hole and binding.Assign/change asset, undo/redo, save/reload, verify only intended instances and physics/render outputs change.
**P1-C**Bond, Skive and Solidify solver semantics; pattern-relative arrangement/point placement.Bond raises locally measured resistance; Skive lowers it; Solidify holds a recorded drape with strength-controlled response; arrangement links survive round trip.
**P2-A**Grading/POM and avatar contexts; right-click Simulation/Wind/2D Window property windows; mesh/Pin advanced contexts.Graded POM reports correct per-size lengths; background routes open settings context; scene objects and pinned geometry never steal pattern properties.
**P2-B**CLO 7.0.242 visual comparison, capturing every conditional accordion and exact width, typography, ordering, default and selection interaction.Screenshot pairs annotated by app version and test project; parity score reported per context, with unknowns explicitly unresolved.

Acceptance test suite: scenarios to automate and visually inspect

IDSetup / actionRequired result
PE-01Select pattern then edge then blank 2D gray.Context changes `pattern → outerEdge → none`; stale lengths and fabric details disappear.
PE-02Select 10 cm draft edge with no 3D correspondence.2D length = 10.00 cm; 3D = unavailable; no copied 10.00 value.
PE-03Select mapped edge, drag fabric with live simulation, compare 2D/3D arclengths.Independent readings and positive/negative elongation update with solver snapshot revision.
PE-04Change display units cm → mm → in.Round-trip values equivalent within display precision; underlying geometry unchanged.
PE-05Edit target 2D specification 0, -2, 1000.01, valid 25.5, then stale revision.Invalid values and stale request rejected; valid edit only changes specification; no false elastic reaction claim.
PE-06Enable Elastic ratio 80% with backed native edge constraint, simulate.Rest target and solver constraints change; actual observable contraction under relevant conditions; undo restores. Gate test until implemented.
PE-07Add tape to edge, adjust width/preset/extension, save/reload.Tape identity and thickness material survive and influence drape; missing intersection disables extension. Gate test until implemented.
PE-08Select sewing pair, adjust tension/fold; toggle Turned.Matching paired seam gets updated; conditional Fold fields disable; all edits persist and undo. Gate test until implemented.
PE-09Edit topstitch asset vs one stitch instance.Style change affects assigned instances; placement/flip change affects chosen instance; cutting-line Flip disabled.
PE-10Select Fabric asset, change physical preset, then optical texture.Physics change influences simulated drape; optical change updates surface without silently altering solver mass or stiffness.
PE-11Select internal shape, apply Bond, Skive or Solidify sequentially.Each action exhibits its distinct physical meaning; unsupported control disabled and explained.
PE-12Grade two sizes; create group POM across two patterns.Length per grade matches traced pattern portions; bridging gap excluded, live 3D length independent.
PE-13Select pattern with no arrangement point, then assign one.Arrangement-point fields appear only after assignment; staging position and point offset never alias.
PE-14Right-click empty 3D and 2D areas.Proper Simulation/Wind vs 2D Window context offered without pattern selection carryover.
PE-15Keyboard navigation, multi-select, reload and undo.Accordion state accessible; mixed values accurate; repeated selection and persisted properties remain consistent.

Remaining local CLO 7.0.242 inspection required

To finish version-specific parity, a reviewer with access to the live installed application should record the selected tool and exact Property Editor screenshot for every row, especially pattern thickness/collision, graphics, detailed physical material coefficients, button/buttonhole defaults, grading, Pin, Avatar, Wind and 2D background. Copy each label, min/max/step, initial value, unit and dependent visibility into the matrix. No such screenshot inspection was completed in this audit, so these rows intentionally retain L? rather than claiming exact CLO layouts or control coverage.

CLO 7.0.242 live Property Editor audit — 2026-10-16

Evidence and observed selection state

This audit uses a real native CLO window capture, not an EXO simulation. The signed EXO CLO Inspector.app wrote ~/Library/Application Support/EXO-CLO-Inspector/clo-property-editor-reference.png and latest-status.json at 2026-10-16 21:32:22 UTC. A copy of the screenshot is docs/clo7-live-property-reference-20261016.png. Its bottom-left status reads Version: 7.0.242 (r39887). The window title is CLO Standalone OnlineAuth Academic; the open file is Default_Modelist.zprj. The helper reports accessibility: true, screenRecording: true, CLO PID 22394, one AX window and no warnings. The AX capture contains named toolbar buttons, but no Property Editor descendants or individual field labels. A successful accessibility permission check does not imply complete AX exposure of the game's custom-rendered editor.

Initial direct visual observations (LIVE, before selection): The right-hand dock has Object Browser above Property Editor, with a distinct separator/header. Object Browser shows one checked, white-swatch FABRIC 1 row. The visible 2D Pattern Window is a blank grid and the 3D viewport contains no garment. In this initial screenshot the Property Editor is blank. A checked fabric row alone does not establish an active property selection. Subsequent approved live captures now demonstrate a successfully selected fabric and populated Property Editor, detailed below. The earlier empty-state evidence remains valid for that earlier state only.

Subsequent live Fabric selection: exact observed rows

Native evidence files: integrations/clothcraft/output/clo3d-reference/clo-after-fabric-click.png (selected FABRIC 1, top of inspector) and integrations/clothcraft/output/clo3d-reference/clo-property-fabric-scroll-deep-4.png (same selected fabric, scrolled into Material). Both screenshots display CLO 7.0.242 (r39887). The Object Browser row is visibly highlighted blue and the right Property Editor is populated with a Fabric context. This is LIVE evidence of a fabric asset selection despite the blank garment views; it supersedes the earlier statement that no fabric-property selection had been seen.
