Source mapping and implementation roadmap

src/components/CloPropertyEditorAccordion.jsx provides the selection-context shell; docs/EXO_CLO3D_V2_PROPERTY_EDITOR_IMPLEMENTATION_20261016.md documents its earlier implementation stage. The prime reports a newer EXO Fabric editor mirroring Information / Material / Physical Property and intentionally disabled independent face controls; this audit records that update without claiming a new EXO screenshot test. docs/CLO3D_PROPERTY_EDITOR_DEEP_AUDIT_20261016.md supplies the official-reference matrix and proposed acceptance cases. Earlier documents asserting no native visual evidence are superseded for both the blank context and the selected Fabric context described here.

  1. P0: empty-state parity. In a V2 browser/native app QA session, select no object and compare the right property body against the new CLO capture; ensure no pattern-specific accordions or stale values leak into an empty selection. Distinguish the currently visible Fabric browser listing from active Fabric *property selection*. Preserve other project controls in their proper location.
  2. P0: complete live selected-context evidence. Fabric asset is now captured and partially mapped. Continue scrolling its Physical Property group and inspect material Reflection subfields, face tab behavior and dropdown choices. Separately select whole pattern, edge, internal line, sewing pair and topstitch style/instance in an expendable CLO test project. Save screenshot and AX JSON *per state* and record exact labels, order, values, units and disabled conditions. Confirm build remains 7.0.242.
  3. P1: schema and parity. Tie each selection kind to a typed field registry with name, type, unit, range, readOnly, condition, capability and native/draft command. Compare observed screenshots with rendered EXO selection states; prioritize pattern/edge/sewing/fabric before minor style contexts.
  4. P1: actual physical behavior. Add solver-backed edge elastic, seam tape and fold/tension; then Bond/Skive/Solidify; verify native changes alter cloth behavior rather than only editor text. Add style-vs-instance separation for topstitch and hardware. Existing official reference ranges are candidates for validation, not proof of identical 7.0.242 defaults.
  5. P2: repeatable evidence. Capture a side-by-side comparison per context with scale, platform, selection ID, active tool and accordion expansion; test edit, undo/redo, reload and unit conversions, including mixed selections and unavailable 3D mappings. Only then assign a version-specific parity score.

Evidence limit: A signed helper successfully selected the live FABRIC 1 asset and captured the two approved screenshots; the audit reads those screenshots. CLO pattern, edge, sewing, topstitch and other selection contexts remain unobserved. No dropdown was expanded in the evidence, no independent face setting was changed, and the visible screenshots do not establish Physical Property coefficients. This documentation update did not alter any CLO garment or EXO application source.

SOURCE ANNEX 06 · DATED EXISTING RESEARCH

EXO_400_FABRIC_LIBRARY_AND_WASH_SYSTEM.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

EXO 400+ Fabric Library and Digital Wash System

Research, implementation and engineering acceptance specification — 16 October 2026. The implemented module references below are verified by a programmatic catalog audit; future high-fidelity features in this document remain proposals. It does not certify that EXO currently has 400 experimentally characterized/photorealistic fabrics or that its implementation matches CLO.

Implemented scope (validated October 16, 2026)

Subsequent real-scan appearance pass on the same 400

All 400 presets were re-audited against actual installed and SHA-256-verified CC0 photographs; the result is in docs/reports/EXO_400_FABRICS_COVERAGE.csv and .json with exactly 400 rows.

Evidence stateActual count
EXO garment fabric presets**400**
Unique installed CC0 photographic PBR source materials**67** (37 Poly Haven, 30 ambientCG)
Unique CC0 sources actually used by presets**44**
Verified installed images (1k JPG albedo, OpenGL normal, roughness)**201**
Fabric presets referencing an installed photographed material**385**
Construction-reference assignments**220**
Related-fabric proxy assignments (NOT an exact fabric scan)**165**
Textile-specific generated appearance fallback (mostly fine/open mesh)**15**
New per-specimen mechanically measured/calibrated materials**0**

Real sources were obtained from official Poly Haven and ambientCG APIs under CC0 licenses (see https://polyhaven.com/license and https://docs.ambientcg.com/books/website-licensing/page/license-information), with SHA-256 checksums, author/source and provider metadata preserved in src/data/polyhavenScans.generated.json and src/data/ambientcgScans.generated.json. No proprietary CLO textures are used. Resumable installers are scripts/install-polyhaven-fabric-scans.py and scripts/install-ambientcg-fabric-scans.py; the combined strict asset registry is docs/reports/EXO_400_LICENSED_SCAN_REGISTRY.json. src/data/polyhavenFabricAssignments.js documents assignment to the 80 base archetypes, inherited by four additional variants; no extra material specimen is created by editing color or GSM.

Photo maps and provider-declared repeat scaling are installed via src/data/polyhavenScans.js and rendered in both browser and native swatches. Some ambientCG assets do not publish physical repeat measurements, so their default repeat scale is marked unknown/illustrative rather than verified. Colorways use non-destructive photographic tonal retargeting: source yarn detail is retained while the user adjusts color and wash strength. The 15 unmatched materials retain procedural textures suited to fine meshes/tulle and unsupported physical constructions (neither substitutes for yarn-level cloth simulation).

Clarification of evidence grade: The strict photo registry may grade a fabric "A" for complete color + normal + roughness maps. That is optical map coverage only. It is not a claim of color-spectrophotometric accuracy, a scan of the exact EXO fabric variation, or laboratory-determined drape/stress data. Related-fabric proxies are clearly separated in the audit. The 1k source maps suit interactive previews; higher-resolution originals may be available for later selective re-import. The two source caches occupy approximately 191 MB (90 MB Poly Haven and 101 MB ambientCG); additional bulk downloading was paused because the Mac had approximately 19 GiB free.

The physical importer in src/core/fabricEvidenceCalibration.js supports traceable mass/thickness measurements and directional laboratory curves with provenance and unit checks. In the editor, use Physical Fabric > Import Lab Specimen Data (JSON) to add *user-provided* specimen data. Areal mass and thickness can be converted directly to the solver's SI quantities; uncalibrated tensile, bending and shear fits remain advisory. A user's submitted file is marked imported-unverified until instrument and specimen traceability are independently confirmed. This is intentionally not an automated 400-material calibration claim.

The visual preview contact sheet generated from all current preset colors and photo references is screenshots/EXO_400_FABRIC_CONTACT_SHEET.jpg. Its JPG thumbnails are graphic previsualizations, not WebGL screenshots or measured color targets. The main and native PBR rendering use the source image, normal and roughness maps.

  • Exactly 400 named, unique, selectable React material records: 80 original archetypes plus 320 physical/finish variants, assembled from src/data/fabricCatalog400.js and src/data/defaultProject.js. Four variants per base change weight, thickness, bending, directional stretch, friction and optical controls—not only color. They are parametric estimates, not 400 unique fabric specimens.
  • 405 Rust fabric choices in src/core/clothcraftFabrics.js: 400 aligned construction IDs plus five extra original instrument/reference specimens retained for backward compatibility. The same underlying material models are not used by the React and Rust solvers; conversion remains approximate.
  • 16 selectable, parameterized digital appearance effects, including modeled enzyme, stone, acid-marble, ozone, bleaching, localized PP-style fading, garment dye, whiskers, seam-edge fading and distress. src/core/clothWashProcedurals.js generates deterministic periodic masks; src/core/clothWashImage.js provides browser sRGB image grading. Project wash metadata is stored on fabric and changes remain optical-only in the main React workflow.
  • src/core/textileProcedurals.js applies color-graded wash to generated yarn/base color maps, while GarmentViewport.jsx and FabricMaterialPreview.jsx also apply visual grading to loaded photographic color maps. The source image itself is not overwritten. Original procedural normal/roughness and uploaded maps remain independent.
  • src/core/clothcraftFabricSurface.js renders matching preconfigured washes on saved native fabric preset names, but the native workspace does not yet offer a persisted arbitrary wash layer editor. Native custom finish stack persistence is a future bridge.
  • Automated tests scripts/check-apparel-catalog.mjs, scripts/check-cloth-washes.mjs, and scripts/check-cloth-wash-image.mjs verify exact catalog size, finite parameters, uniqueness, deterministic tiling and reversible unmodified source RGB. npm run build and npm run check:materials passed. Physical drape certification, full-page visual regression, manufacturer wash QA and per-panel seam masks are not established by these checks.
  • Native exhaustive simulation fixture integrations/clothcraft/check-v2-fabrics.mjs is time-intensive at 405 materials. Run EXO_FABRIC_QA_LIMIT=20 node integrations/clothcraft/check-v2-fabrics.mjs for a deterministic distributed sample plus high-priority denim/terry/leather/suede variants (27 samples), which passed material assignment, saved reopen, undo/redo and finite native steps. Pure consistency tests validate all 405 material descriptors, but the 405-case full native simulation exercise was terminated for runtime cost and must not be reported as passed.

Quality classification of the new 320 variants: Grade C/D *estimated construction descriptors and generated visuals*, zero newly instrument-measured textile specimens. A/B measured-fabric targets below are future quality gates; showing a count of 400 must never imply calibrated fabrics or scanned material photos.

Verified reference and scope

The official CLO Express Denim Wet Wash guide (updated 19 August 2026; feature introduced in CLO 2025.1) says to select Material Type: Denim, supply a denim Base Color Map, then choose a wash effect. CLO describes the effects as changes to the fabric's Base Color Map. The eight published presets are Unwashed, Rinse, Enzyme, Stone, Acid, Bleach, Ozone, Custom. CLO's stated controls are: Wash Map concentration/intensity, Transformation (scale, rotation, translation), Blend (with Overlay recommended for many denim washes), Absorbency, Cycle Duration, and Seam Abrasion (modifies appearance associated with pattern puckering). The article documents categories and qualitative behavior, not precise numerical control ranges, pixel algorithms, fabric test measurements, or a reproducible CLO rendering baseline. Accordingly, EXO should expose independently designed functionality and use its own names and algorithms rather than infer unpublished CLO internal formulas.

The related official CLO Material Type article describes Denim alongside fabric reflection/material types. Source reference is for comparative research only; no CLO screenshots, source images, procedural shader code, proprietary assets, or wash maps are to be copied.

Proposed EXO system architecture

Maintain three linked but independently versioned records:

  1. Fabric master: unique stable ID, human-readable name, construction/fiber composition, nominal GSM, measured or estimated thickness, warp/weft grain, mechanical calibration with evidence level, optical PBR family, base color/texture, yarn-scale texture repeat, licenses and source provenance. A master is a real fabric category/specimen variant, not a colorway or wash alias.
  2. Fabric appearance variant: parent fabric ID, colorway, UV repeat, base PBR maps and an ordered, editable digital finish stack. Store original map references and parameters; regenerate derivative textures without overwriting originals. Share finish templates between fabrics but keep per-variant inputs independent.
  3. Panel assignment: pattern panel ID, parent fabric/variant ID, stable grainline, UV transform, panel-specific masking, seam references, optional overrides. Persist alignment to the 2D pattern as well as the simulated 3D garment; avoid binding wear marks to arbitrary world-space coordinates.

Suggested asset graph:

FabricMaster(id, mechanics, PBR, evidence)
  -> AppearanceVariant(id, base maps, finishStack[])
  -> PanelAssignment(panelId, uvLayout, grainline)
  -> SeamTopology(pairings, stitch lines, allowances, puckering)
  -> TextureBake(albedo, roughness, normal?, height?, AO?)
  -> Browser/Native render + swatch + BOM/techpack export

An illustrative finish-layer schema (a *design target*, not a claim of existing EXO API) is:

{
  "id": "finish-unique-id",
  "kind": "denim-wash",
  "preset": "stone",
  "enabled": true,
  "seed": 31415,
  "strength": 0.45,
  "durationVisual": 0.5,
  "spread": 0.35,
  "blendMode": "overlay",
  "transform": {"offsetU": 0, "offsetV": 0, "scaleU": 1, "scaleV": 1, "rotationDeg": 0},
  "maskAssetId": null,
  "seamAbrasion": 0.4,
  "edgeWear": 0.3,
  "reversible": true
}

Normalize every numeric field to a published EXO range and reject nonfinite values. The 0–1 values above are proposed UI conventions, not official CLO ranges. Preserve seed, implementation version, color space, inputs and checksums so exports can be reproduced. Finish edits must participate in undo/redo, versions, named colorways, manufacturer comments and export metadata.

Denim wash preset targets: appearance only

Digital presetVisible result to targetProposed masks and texture response
Unwashed / rawPreserved dark, saturated dye, sharp twill reliefIdentity base color; no wash mask
RinseSubtle evenness and small reduction in apparent saturationLow-amplitude broad mask, minimal localized contrast
Enzyme-inspiredGentle broken-in fading with soft transitionsLow-frequency attenuation plus mild raised-area wear
Stone-inspiredUneven highlights, pronounced seam/edge wearMulti-scale wear noise, fold-ridge and edge masks
Acid-inspiredHigh-contrast irregular cloud/marble patternSeeded clustered mask with smooth/fragmented boundaries
Bleach-inspiredLocalized to broad color lighteningControlled pigment-removal color blend, preserve yarn detail
Ozone-inspiredGentle fairly uniform fadeBroad low-contrast fading mask
CustomUser-owned photography or authored masksEditable map transforms, channel blending and intensity

These labels describe visual approximations. A digital filter does not chemically change dyestuff, predict process chemistry, validate environmental impact, soften a real fabric, or prove garment longevity. Even when the appearance suggests a softer hand, solver properties stay fixed until separately provided with measured post-treatment mechanical values and explicit user approval to change the simulated fabric.

Extend finishing beyond denim through the same stack: pigment or garment-dye look, vintage wash, sunfade, localized whiskers, honeycombs, knee/seat wear, edge abrasion, soft overdyed patina, uneven dye uptake, suede nap direction variation, leather patina and highlight wear, cotton/fleece garment aging, and print crackle. Use applicable masks per material; do not imply that every wet process is valid for leather, silk, wool or treated composites. Names such as 'potassium wash' should be presented as visual looks only, with no chemical formulations, process recipes, concentrations, times, temperatures or manufacturing operating instructions.

Correct placement on UVs, patterns, and stitches

UV invariants. Generate primary repeat UVs from each 2D panel in centimeters. Keep fabric twill/knit orientation registered to grainline. On a stitched 3D garment, the mask must follow simulated vertex UVs during motion and deformation. Pattern resizing and grading must regenerate appropriate UV coordinates and remap authored local mask landmarks. Preserve authored offsets and seam continuity when panels are split or re-sewn.

Construction-aware masks. Derive edge-distance maps from valid pattern boundary edges, separately tagging fold, hem, cut, bound, zipper, pocket, waistband, seam allowance and internal stitch lines. Use signed or unsigned distance in physical centimeters, accounting for texel density. A fade near a hem should remain similar physical width on differently sized panels. Use user controls for seam bias and localized exclusion masks; avoid automatic artificial bright seams on raw or taped, sealed, reversible or non-denim materials.

Seam registration. Sewn edges refer to paired pattern edge IDs and correspondence/arclength. Edge-wear and puckering patterns should interpolate along those paths, with orientation and stitch density attached to the seam. Avoid accidental discontinuities when a wash crosses front/back joins; intentional panel discontinuities are allowed as a design choice. For whiskering, offer user-placed anchor areas at hip/upper thigh with directional fold curves and displacement from avatar pose, then bake on the panel UVs. Seat/knee worn regions should use labeled user-adjustable pattern zones, not hardcoded pixel rectangles that shift with size.

Map pipeline. Base color in sRGB; scalar/normal/height masks in non-color data space. Perform blending in a documented linear-light workflow and convert for storage/viewing as needed. Preserve weave and twill contrast by altering dye/pigment appearance without deleting the entire yarn normal map. Anti-alias masks, generate mip levels, check UV seams at render distance, and use a deterministic fallback when a map is absent. Roughness or normal response may be altered in an independent optical layer where appropriate; no direct change to collision thickness, mass, bending stiffness, tensile stiffness or friction from a color-only wash.

Recommended generation dependency graph: finish params -> UV/edge/zone masks -> dye/fade color composite -> optional optical map composite -> mipmapped render assets. Cache by fabric variant, panel geometry/UV revision, seam topology revision, finish version, input image hash and deterministic seed. A color-only operation must not reset simulated particle positions or rewrite garment pattern geometry.

Measured physics versus visual plausibility

Current project reference paths include src/core/clothcraftFabrics.js, src/data/apparelMaterialExpansion.js, src/core/fabricSurfaceModel.js, src/core/fabricCalibration.js, src/core/textileProcedurals.js, src/core/fabricPbrGenerator.js, src/components/ClothCraftFabricLibrary.jsx, and src/components/FabricMaterialPreview.jsx. Existing source already distinguishes approximate material appearances from mechanical inputs; a source comment in clothcraftFabrics.js notes measured mass/thickness for some specimens but estimated elasticity and bending. fabricCalibration.js fits elementary measured tensile responses, not a complete fabric constitutive model. The [existing EXO materials reference](EXO_FABRIC_MATERIALS_AND_TRIMS.md) documents normal-map and shader limitations.

Physical fidelity requires specimen-level evidence for areal density (g/m²), thickness (mm with defined pressure), warp/weft extension versus applied load, shear, bend hysteresis, damping, surface friction, compression, shrinkage and behavior after real finishing when relevant. Record test method, specimen conditioning, force/displacement curve, uncertainty, and solver-specific fit/error. For denim, compare raw and physically finished *measured* samples before offering 'wash changed hand feel' simulations. An artist-made appearance may still be visually convincing without these measurements: label that honestly as optically authored / mechanics estimated.

Practical simulation gate: identical garment, poses, timestep and initial conditions must show unchanged cloth positions after an appearance-only edit within stated floating-point tolerance; independently applied measured mechanical updates should alter corresponding cloth observations when re-simulated. Test UV stability on a moving avatar and cloth self-contact; shader displacement and painted creases do not prove actual folded topology, abrasion damage or additional collision geometry.

Swatch photography and texture capture protocol

  1. Obtain a physical reference swatch with recorded consent/license, manufacturer/lot, fiber content, weave/knit, nominal GSM, finishing history, dimensions and measurements. Capture raw and finished variants when available. Do not assign a chemical-process label solely from visual similarity.
  2. Condition and flatten a representative area consistently; capture a measured centimeter scale, neutral gray reference, calibrated color target and fiber/grain direction indicator. For PBR, obtain high-resolution macro photographs and garment-scale images under stable diffuse illumination. Include raking light and cross/polarized lighting when equipment allows; document the lighting and lens profile.
  3. Capture controlled front/back albedo, weave/nap relief, low-angle directional reflection and transmitted-light reference when relevant (especially sheer cloth). For pile materials, photograph multiple fiber orientations because velvet, suede and brushed fleece change substantially with viewing direction.
  4. Correct distortion and perspective against the scale marker, white-balance/color-manage with chart profile, document RGB working space, remove illumination gradients without erasing material texture and create edge-compatible tile crops. Do not mistake shaded wrinkles for diffuse pigmentation.
  5. Derive roughness, normal and displacement maps through separately validated imaging or artist calibration. A single diffuse photo cannot uniquely recover physical roughness, normal microgeometry or BRDF. Mark inferred maps estimated. Set physical repeats in millimeters/centimeters and use matching UV axes.
  6. Compare the rendered flat swatch, sphere/macro sample and full draped garment under matching reference studio lights at near and far distances. Save baseline captures with color target, input image hashes, source rights, calibration notes and compare dates. Use a calibrated display for judgment; no screen preview promises production dye match.

Capture minimum for priority families: rigid/stretch denim (face/back), cotton jersey, French terry (loop/back), fleece, poplin, canvas, twill, leather, suede, silk satin, wool suiting, linen, nylon ripstop, rib knit, velvet, mesh and sheer organza. Build family-specific test variants: black/white/high-chroma dyes, fine/coarse weave, shallow/deep relief, heavy/light weights, brushed and coated finishes, cross-grain orientation.

400+ catalog acceptance

Count definition: target >= 400 unique meaningful fabric masters identified by stable IDs and differing in material composition, construction, weight, finish substrate or other defensible physical/material identity. Multiple dye colors, repeated presets, slight noise seeds, wash look variants, or near-identical parameter clones do not increase the unique-master count. Keep appearance variants separate; they may number in the thousands but must never inflate the underlying 400 claim.

Coverage plan (targets, not verified current counts):

**Total planned****412**All category counts require machine-audited distinct source entries

Run a full automated catalog audit on the actual assembled runtime catalog, not source-file text counts. Assert 400+ stable unique IDs and names; valid, normalized numeric optics and finite physical data with units; correct family assignment; fabric master ≠ colorway ≠ wash variant; deterministic import/export; editable controls that persist after reopening; successful fabric assignment to an actual 2D panel and visible 3D mesh. Deduplicate case/spacing aliases and compare fingerprints (fiber composition, knit/weave, GSM band, thickness, stretch profile, optic family), flag suspicious near-identical records for manual review.

For each master, validate 1) source/provenance and license metadata, 2) useful swatch/fallback, 3) color map plus calibrated or explicitly estimated roughness/normal/relief, 4) consistent cm UV scale and grain direction, 5) nonzero credible physical metadata and its evidence status, 6) serialized save/load and production data export, 7) viewport application and 8) accessible search/filter/grouping. Quality is graded separately: A specimen-measured mechanical + color-calibrated optical reference; B part-measured with optical reference; C literature-estimated and artist-generated; D placeholder. Report A/B/C/D counts; a 400 total with 390 placeholders fails the intended quality bar.

For denim finishes, minimum golden scenes: raw indigo, sulfur black, light washed blue, heavyweight/slubby twill, stretch denim, jean front with fly/pockets/hem, jean back with seat/pockets, jacket with yoke and cuffs. Snapshot all eight presets across representative scenes and compare a physical/photo reference where licensed. Include intensity endpoints, UV transform rotations and scales, independent seed determinism, blend selection, area masks, seam abrasion at three levels, extreme panel aspect ratios, resizes/grading, symmetry, undo/redo, round-trip save/load, 3D pose motion, lighting changes and distance mipmapping. In particular assert finish edits do not modify recorded physical solver state.

Visual QA must catch: faded areas that drift in world space during cloth motion; identical wear printed onto both legs despite different panel mappings; seams glowing uniformly despite absence of puckering; twill detail disappearing under strong bleach looks; unlicensed photographs shipped with default assets; wash texture repeats exposing obvious tile seams; albedo treated as data or normal maps treated as sRGB; progress UI announcing '400 realistic fabrics' with no verified runtime total. Preserve separate measured optical similarity scores and solver fitting errors where evidence exists.

Suggested release gates: catalog count and integrity tests green; every entry can be assigned and reopened; each family has a documented manual lighting/visual sample; denim golden scenes reviewed; no catastrophic UV seams in sampled garments; wash stack exports are deterministic and reversible; no unlicensed reference maps; calibrated-vs-estimated badges visible; measured physics claims limited to measured specimens. Establish tolerances with baseline image differences and panel displacement fixtures rather than inventing unverified universal pixel/perceptual thresholds.

Product semantics, rights, and safety

Label interface controls Digital Wash / Finish Appearance, with a separate optional Measured Post-Finish Mechanics asset only when actual tests support it. Store wash settings and reference image provenance in editable techpacks: finished-look target, source fabric, colorway, layers, reference photographs, panel mask placements, revision notes and manufacturer comments. Export swatches and a visual spec with a clear disclaimer that manufacturing results require separate factory sampling and approval.

Accept user-owned or appropriately licensed swatches and original procedural generators; track source URL, creator, license, attribution restrictions and whether redistribution/derivatives are allowed. Avoid bundling CLO media, logos, proprietary textures and reverse-engineered code. Never offer operational chemical wash formulas or infer worker/environmental safety from a rendered wash effect. Physical production instructions should be written only from qualified manufacturing documentation and applicable safety standards outside this digital appearance specification.

All proposed fields and targets remain unimplemented unless separately verified by code and tests.
SOURCE ANNEX 07 · DATED EXISTING RESEARCH

FABRIC_PHYSICS_AND_CLO_RESEARCH_2026-10-16.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

EXO ClothCraft V2 — Fabric science, CLO feature reference, calibration and rendering

Research date: 2026-10-16. Scope: public CLO documentation, textile measurement standards, graphics practice and a read-only EXO source inspection. Evidence convention: “documented CLO behavior” means CLO's published UI/test behavior; “EXO observation” means the inspected repository; “proposed” means engineering guidance; “estimate” means an unverified starting point. Neither published CLO physics presets nor calibration internals are copied here.

1. Summary of findings

Fabric realism has two coupled but independently testable systems: (1) physical dynamics, whose key inputs are areal mass, anisotropic tensile extension, shear, bending, thickness/collision, friction and energy dissipation, and (2) optical response, which depends on yarn-scale pattern, normal/height, roughness, reflectance, fibers, translucency, side-specific appearance and lights. A physically correct drape can look synthetic if its fiber surface is flat; a photorealistic swatch can still behave like cardboard.

CLO provides configurable fabric physical properties and material maps. Its CLOFAB interface (current published guidance dated September 2026) supports a fabric type, composition and density workflow, adjustable advanced physical properties, generated/scanned PBR maps, preview on cylinder or dummy, and saving fabric with textures. Its legacy Fabric Kit documents weighing, thickness gauging, warp/weft/bias bending and stretch testing. These are measurement and workflow references, not evidence that EXO uses CLO's proprietary model or its calibration data.

References: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/49343947711769-CLOFAB ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041074334-Fabric-Kit-Manual ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000483087-Adjust-Stretch-Weft-Warp-Shear

2. CLO: what is confirmed publicly

  • Physical axis directions: CLO names Stretch-Weft, Stretch-Warp and Shear in Fabric > Property Editor > Physical Property > Detail. CLO's guidance says increasing shear resistance changes how easily cotton/denim crease, while softer silk/jersey can use lower resistance. Do not map those slider numbers directly to EXO N/m without an identified calibration experiment. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000483087-Adjust-Stretch-Weft-Warp-Shear
  • Legacy Fabric Kit: public instructions describe cutting prescribed swatches, weighing multiple samples, thickness using a gauge, three bending orientations (weft, warp, bias) with geometry readings and a stretch force device. Follow the exact instrument manual for swatch sizes/loads rather than improvising its calibration formula. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041074334-Fabric-Kit-Manual
  • CLOFAB (2026): catalog type/composition/density create an initial behavior, with Advanced Details to inspect/edit. Image maps may be captured or scanned, imported in coordinated filename sets, and saved to a fabric file with available textures. The workflow previews drape as cylinder circle/square or dummy cape. CLO describes integration with its zFab Kit; this does not imply universal interchangeability of measurements from other kits. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/49343947711769-CLOFAB
  • Material channels: CLO lists base color, normal, displacement, opacity and reflection-related controls; material appearance can be configured for front/back/side, with front/back different when same-material sharing is disabled. Its PBR map generation covers normal, displacement, opacity, roughness and metalness. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360047317454-Set-Fabric-Materials ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/33852438561049-PBR-Map-Generator-ver-2024-1
  • Height vs simulator geometry: CLO's displacement support documents render-only effects in the cited workflow. EXO should likewise explicitly distinguish render displacement from actual collision thickness, unless a physics collision implementation is verified. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360025439933-Displacement-Map-ver5-1-0
  • Accuracy claims: CLO's public FAQ states approximately “90% accuracy” for its own library/Fabric Kit fabrics and cautions arbitrary changes can affect it. That statement has no publicly defined error metric or universal transfer claim; EXO must report its own test-specific measured errors. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/115013660747-How-accurate-are-virtual-fabrics
  • CLO Fabric Guide: official downloadable fabric guide is linked from CLO support and discusses physical properties/textures. It is useful for UI terminology, not a license to redistribute private presets. Source: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360001436227-CLO-Fabric-Guide

3. Canonical EXO SI schema and governing relationships

Use measured material records with unit-tagged parameters and full provenance. Store scalar values in SI and convert solely in UI. Recommended representation:

QuantityCanonical storageDisplayMethod / model meaning
Areal mass `rhoA`kg/m²g/m² (GSM)dry conditioned measured swatch mass divided by area
Physical thickness `t`mmmmeasured at **specified compression pressure**; physical collision thickness separate
Extensional stiffness `Kw,Kf`N/mN/mtangent/secant membrane force per width versus engineering strain, at recorded strain/loading
Shear stiffness `G`N/mN/mfor dimensionless engineering shear strain; state convention/angle
Bending rigidity `Dw,Df`N·mµN·mshell plate/membrane energy per area convention; unit depends on exact discretization
Fiber direction `theta`raddegreeswarp/wale local rotation with UV/pattern pieces
Dampingmodel-dependentunit-taggedviscous, Rayleigh, or dimensionless per-step ratio must be distinguished
Surface friction `mu`dimensionlessdimensionlessstatic/kinetic, cloth-cloth and cloth-avatar tested separately
Rest/locked strainsratio%shrinkage and imposed residual strain must not be confused
Optical roughness, metalness, sheendimensionless0–1shader parameters, **not** textile mechanical roughness

Conversion: rhoA[kg/m²] = GSM/1000, t[m]=thicknessMm/1000, D[N·m]=valueInMicroNewtonMeter × 1e-6. Example: 460 GSM = 0.460 kg/m²; 1.2 mm = 0.0012 m. Gravitation load per actual fabric area: q = rhoA × g N/m², with g ≈ 9.81 m/s².

Strain and energy, illustrative continuum model: engineering strain epsilon=(L-L0)/L0 is dimensionless; surface force per width N=K epsilon (N/m) only for a locally linear tensile region; energy per undeformed surface area U_stretch≈0.5 K epsilon² (J/m²). For anisotropic fabrics use U≈1/2 [Kw epsilon_w² + Kf epsilon_f² + G gamma²], with extra coupling/nonlinear recovery terms when calibrated. For bending with curvature kappa (1/m), U_bend≈1/2 D (kappa-kappa0)² (J/m²), hence D dimensions N·m. The discrete solver's spring stiffness must be derived from mesh spacing and its specific energy; inserting continuum values blindly changes behavior with tessellation.

Dynamics: M x_ddot + C x_dot + grad(E_stretch+E_shear+E_bend+E_contact+E_seam)=f_external. Cloth is often nonlinear, especially knit, bias and leather. Compression buckles instead of sharing tensile stiffness; use one-sided tensile response or calibrated buckling. XPBD compliance has dimensions/solver scaling different from stiffness: convert using the implementation's constraint energy and time step, then verify time step and mesh convergence. Friction coefficients belong in the collision/contact model and are distinct from a fabric PBR roughness map.

Caution: stretchWarp:0.12 in defaultProject.js is a ratio-like authoring field, while warp in ClothCraft's solver record is N/m. They cannot be assigned to each other by name. Bending coefficients in the default project's bend:0.68 are authoring/normalized controls and are not N·m. Mechanical simulation thickness should be tested for how collisions actually use the parameter.

4. EXO implementation audit as of inspection

Inspected pathEvidence in codeGap / action
`src/data/defaultProject.js`Initial presets jersey, heavy French terry, rigid denim, silk and interfacing plus poplin/canvas/wool/leather/suede/nylon/linen/knits/velvet/organza/corduroy variants; fields include GSM, `stretchWarp`, `bend`, `thickness`, PBR controls. `src/core/clothcraftFabrics.js`Ten material examples with `gsm`, `thicknessMm`, extension %, `shear`, two `bending` directions, source/evidence; `presetProperties` populates `density`, `thickness`, `warp`, `weft`, `shear`, `bend_warp`, `bend_weft`. `FABRIC_FIELDS` lists display conversions.Some specimen mass/thickness from MIT research dataset, but stiffness and many optical values are **estimates**. No source inspection by itself proves the cloth solver reproduces laboratory tensile/shear curves, geometric pile, leather hysteresis, fabric self-contact under load, or all maps in live garment rendering. Those require the tests below.

5. Measurement and calibration protocol

  1. Identify each physical sample: manufacturer, SKU/lot, fiber %, blend, weave/knit type, finish, washed/unwashed, actual dye shade, fabric face/back, sample orientation and date. Condition swatches under a specified measured humidity and temperature; record them for repeatability.
  2. Measure areal mass and thickness: cut multiple known-area swatches, weigh with calibrated scale, measure under stated compressive force/foot area. Save mean, standard deviation and raw samples; thickness is pressure dependent particularly for terry, fleece and suede.
  3. Tensile and recovery: measure warp/weft (or wale/course) force-versus-extension at *several* low-strain loads, then unload/reload to measure hysteresis and residual strain. A single “stretch %” without force and recovery is insufficient.
  4. Bias and shear: conduct bias-extension or dedicated shear rig with specimen geometry, pretension and angle recorded. Calibrate against observed folds, not the texture image. Distinguish bias extension from a pure-shear modulus.
  5. Bending: separate warp/weft/bias cantilever or low-force tester; capture bend length, fold direction and recovery. ASTM D1388 measures bending length/flexural rigidity. For napped/coated textiles, compare face-in/face-out if asymmetric.
  6. Contact: inclined-plane or pull tests for fabric/fabric and fabric/skin surrogate under known load; record static, kinetic, grain-direction and fabric-back differences. Collar/hood contact is sensitive to friction.
  7. Validation geometry: circular drape over pedestal, 20–30 cm hanging strip, controlled bias-square, fold compression test and a standard T-shirt/sweatshirt on a fixed avatar. Record camera, lighting, scale, swatch orientation, garment size and sewing.
  8. Fit parameters: optimize mass/thickness directly from measurement; fit directional tensile/shear curves before bending and damping, then tune contact friction. Use held-out drape tests to check against overfitting; never optimize appearance parameters to disguise mechanical errors.
  9. Quantify accuracy: report silhouette mean boundary distance (mm), static hem depth (mm), curvature distribution, number and wavelength of folds, shoulder/chest strain, dynamic oscillation decay (s), penetration counts/depth (mm), and runtime. Compare same avatar, gravity, mesh resolution, seam pattern and camera.
  10. Convergence & reproducibility: test mesh spacing (e.g. 20, 10, 5 mm), time step and solver iterations; record build commit, machine, physics backend, parameter file and random seed. Values should remain within selected tolerances after refinement.

Standards/references: https://store.astm.org/standards/d1388 ; https://textiles.ncsu.edu/tpacc/comfort-performance/kawabata-evaluation-system/ ; https://research.hva.nl/en/publications/white-paper-ieee-industry-connections-ieee-ic-the-measurement-of-/ ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041074334-Fabric-Kit-Manual

Proposed acceptance targets (engineering goals, NOT achieved results)
  • Schema round-trip: no unit loss; mechanically identical after save/load to within 1e-6 relative for numeric SI values.
  • Uniform-cloth swatch gravity: measured areal mass within scale uncertainty; static silhouettes target ≤ 10 mm mean deviation on controlled mid-size swatches after calibration.
  • Directional tests: reproduce force-vs-extension within 15% at defined strain points where instrument uncertainty permits, and report outliers; at least three repeats per orientation.
  • Bend and folds: target 15% error in mean bend length and fold wavelength; explicitly compare warp and weft.
  • Mesh convergence: selected observable drift ≤ 5% between the two finest practical discretizations; no explosive motion or systematic penetration.
  • Render: check scale accuracy to 1 mm over 100 mm of fabric detail, no base-color or normal map seams under standardized grazing light. Visual review remains necessary.

Targets can be tightened after collecting actual cloth and experimental evidence. Relative thresholds are not claims about achieved commercial accuracy.

6. Material-specific physical and rendering requirements

Cotton plain weave / poplin: relatively low extension on yarn axis, larger diagonal shear; visible spun fiber irregularity and balanced fine plain weave. Use actual yarn count/repeat scale; natural matte diffuse reflectance and modest fiber sheen. Typical use cases: poplin shirts, canvas garments, oxford. Canvas generally requires greater areal mass and bending stiffness than poplin, but values depend strongly on finishing and weight.

Denim (rigid and stretch): anisotropic warp-faced twill; indigo warp / pale weft color separation is critical in closeups. Warp/weft/bias stiffness and directional fold response; rigid variants generally extend less than elastane stretch variants. Render diagonal ribs, subtle warp slubs, seam abrasion, fading concentrated around creases and garment stitching. Texture wear maps should be aligned to the pattern grain, never generated randomly per triangle. Stretch denim must have measured recovery and time-dependent behavior; do not assume any denim preset describes every wash.

French terry / fleece: knit jersey face plus loop pile backside (or brushed fleece); higher loft and compressibility, directional stretch and recovery, surface friction dependent on side. Represent face and backside separately, back loop normals/height, thick hemlines, ribbed cuffs and realistic compression around seams. A flat single normal on both sides will misrepresent this fabric. Measure thickness under load; pile optical displacement cannot replace bulk contact mechanics.

Jersey / rib knit / interlock: more anisotropic extension and nonlinear elastic recovery than typical woven fabrics. Jersey stitch and rib wales need real physical repeat. Ribbed cuffs recover elastically and contract under extension; add calibrated stitch/cuff rest dimensions, not shader-only ridges. Simple membrane shell does not resolve individual knit loops; microstructure is approximated through effective anisotropic constitutive behavior.

Wool suiting / melton: wool suiting has variable bending and recovery due to weave and pressing. Melton is densely felted/fuller with soft diffuse surface and thick edge. Highlight fiber flyaway and subtle broad sheen; avoid aggressive large-scale normal noise. Coats may need laminated/interfacing zones.

Leather (nappa, lambskin, cowhide): tanned hide is not a woven cloth. It may have substantial nonlinear stiffness, anisotropy, plastic set, grain-dependent folding, limited rebound and material-specific friction. Treat top grain, finish, pores, creases, natural variation and cut direction as independent data; separate smooth finished top face from flesh side. Shader clearcoat describes coatings, not a universal “leather gloss.” A generic thin-shell solver approximates leather response but does not simulate damage, cracking, delamination or permanent creasing until explicitly implemented.

Suede / nubuck: napped fiber response, soft broad specular lobes, highly directional visible nap and color change with brushing. Use tangent-aligned fiber anisotropy, micro-normal structure, fine fuzz/sheen, direction-sensitive reflectance and a face/back distinction. Artificially raising roughness to 1 without nap direction is insufficient. Suede thickness and friction depend on surface/finish and conditioning.

Silk satin / twill / chiffon / organza: satin highlights require correct low-frequency anisotropic reflectance orientation and small-scale yarn maps. Chiffon/organza need appropriate porosity/opacity, two-sided shading, thin silhouette treatment and collision separate from transparency. Organza can be optically sheer yet mechanically comparatively crisp. Do not infer bending stiffness from transparency.

Nylon, ripstop, softshell: capture ripstop reinforcement cell pitch, coated-surface specular and dye variation. Lightweight mass does not imply a universal low bending value (coatings alter folds). Softshell may be multi-layer; effective bulk properties should be measured for the stack. Rain beading/wetness is optional optical behavior, independent from PBR dry fabric and cannot prove waterproofness.

Velvet / corduroy: fiber pile anisotropic lobe and fold-dependent shade changes. Corduroy directional wales should have physical pitch; velvet piles need view-angle-dependent fiber shading. Thick pile collision is an approximation unless contact geometry includes loft.

Interfacing / padding / bonded composites: use explicit layered laminate material properties (areal mass total, directional rigidities, adhesive coupling, thickness), bonding location and seam constraints. For trapunto padded hoodie segments, panel-scale quilt stitches and internal loft controls are required; optical bump alone cannot reproduce puff volume or compression.

Hardware: metal buttons, snaps, eyelets and rivets need real object geometry, attachment constraints, collision and correct metal/roughness workflows. Zippers need tape stiffness, tooth/slider geometry, seam coupling and state (open vs closed). Plastic button transmissivity and brushed metal anisotropy are appearance controls. Hardware mass and pendulum motion must be modeled separately from the cloth. Distinguish rendered trims from verified functional physics.

7. Optical map pipeline and QA

Capture: photograph/scans with a ruler, diffuse cross-polarized albedo capture where possible, and multi-directional directional lighting for height/normal reconstruction. Include face/back swatches, color checker and neutral white balance. Store ICC/color-space provenance, sample dimensions, texture repeats and rights/source. A photo under directional light already includes shadows and highlights, so directly using it as base color tends to double-light the fabric.

Channel semantics:

  • Base color/albedo: sRGB texture transformed into linear working space; remove baked specular/shadow and retain authentic yarn color variation.
  • Normal: tangent-space linear map, document OpenGL/DirectX green channel polarity, ensure grain direction transforms correctly with 2D panel rotations/mirror/UV.
  • Height/displacement: linear meters or millimeters per authored amplitude, visual geometry only unless solver explicitly uses it; verify edges and silhouette.
  • Roughness / metalness / ambient occlusion / opacity: linear scalar textures, calibrated roughness distribution, metalness ~0 for uncoated textiles. Metallic threads and metal studs are separate materials. AO shouldn't darken base color twice; opacity can cause depth sorting issues.
  • Sheen / anisotropy / clearcoat: artist controls with material-specific justification; physically sensible fiber orientation varies over cut pieces and backfaces.
  • Maps at scale: store real-world texelsPerMeter or repeatWidthM/repeatHeightM. Clothing pattern UVs represent centimeters, not arbitrary model-scale repetition. Add seam-consistent UV orientation and per-panel grain transform.

Rendering tests: calibrated neutral HDRI and raking side light, daylight plus tungsten, rotating turntable, light/dark swatches, flat vs draped images, 1 cm and 10 cm closeups; white diffuse sphere plus gray reference; normal-only and roughness-only debug. Compare live viewport, fabric preview, export (Blender/glTF, if supported) and any native renderer for parity. Check glTF channel conventions before exporting. Avoid using random tiled high-frequency normals that shimmer at distance: mipmapping, anisotropic filtering and correct LOD are essential.

Official optical sources: https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360047317454-Set-Fabric-Materials ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/33852438561049-PBR-Map-Generator-ver-2024-1 ; https://support.clo3d.com/hc/en-us/articles/360025439933-Displacement-Map-ver5-1-0

8. Recommended engineering milestones

M1 — provenance and units. Define MaterialSpec v1: unique IDs, family, face/back materials, areal density, measured thickness and pressure, warp/weft/shear force curves, directional bending, static/dynamic friction, grain angle, source license, measured/estimated flag and uncertainty. Keep a versioned adapter from editor presets and ClothCraft native fabrics. No silent unit coercion.

M2 — sample-driven physics. Acquire/populate 2–3 specimens each of jersey, heavyweight French terry, rigid/stretch denim, garment nappa leather, suede, canvas, wool and nylon. Produce measured low-strain curves. Extend solver constitutive support only after baseline comparison; check mesh dependence and fabric hand interaction against test strips.

M3 — optical texture library. Capture 4K+ (where justified by physical repeat, not resolution alone) tileable face/back swatches; author true-scale PBR and nap-direction fields. Maintain distinct geometry, physics and render asset references. Add glTF and Blender material conversion tests.

M4 — trims and multilayers. Validate attachment, fabric-hand dragging, buttons and zipper tape on garments. Add lining, interfacing and padding behavior, then quantify collision/contact. Record unresolved constraints on the project progress hub.

M5 — garment reference benchmark. Photograph physical T-shirt, French terry hoodie, denim jacket and leather panel garment on matched form; simulate same authored patterns and published measurement records. Compare drape and render independently, then run controlled user visual reviews.

9. Documentation/source and licensing cautions

A KES system measures bending, tensile, compression and shear, but its result units and acquisition conventions are not automatically compatible with a CLO Fabric Kit, other commercial solvers or EXO. An IEEE/HvA comparison reviews the shortcomings of cross-system fabric measurement and highlights the need for standardization. See: https://textiles.ncsu.edu/tpacc/comfort-performance/kawabata-evaluation-system/ ; https://research.hva.nl/en/publications/white-paper-ieee-industry-connections-ieee-ic-the-measurement-of-/ .

For research specimens and sample metadata, EXO cites the MIT material dataset (https://people.csail.mit.edu/klbouman/pw/projects/materialproperties/dataset.html). Read its reuse terms and actual measurement rows before republishing data. EXO's clothcraftFabrics.js states clearly that a number of mass/thickness values have a measured source but elasticity/bending and optics are estimated; retaining that distinction is mandatory.

CLO's UI, article descriptions and public fabric science inform functional requirements; proprietary presets, kit conversion algorithms, textures and other restricted assets are not assumed accessible or reusable. No numerical EXO simulation error or fabric match is claimed here without sample tests and repeatable outputs.

EXO_PPF_MATERIALS_IMMERSIVE_STUDIOS_FUTURE_ARCHITECTURE_2026-10-16.md

Audit interpretation: This annex preserves earlier engineering research for traceability. Read reported pass counts and statuses as of its original date only. The current 70-slot and 400-fabric inventories above supersede any older source totals.

EXO: PPF Physics, Measured Textiles, PBR Materials, and Immersive Studios

Engineering research extension | 2026-10-16 | FUTURE PLANNING ONLY

Program navigation: [Integrated ecosystem implementation program](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRAM_2026-10-16.md) and [PPH/MAT/STU/COM candidate engineering issues](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_CANDIDATE_ENGINEERING_BACKLOG_2026-10-16.md).

This document develops the next EXO Studio concept: fast garment editing, offline PPF reference contact, real material calibration, realistic textile surfaces, photography studios, and an explorable room of the user's clothing collection. None is requested to be implemented in this turn. Do not alter the running V2 app, original garment projects, native solver, or local PPF jobs.

Companions: [Spatial master research](EXO_SPATIAL_PROJECTION_GARMENT_STUDIO_AND_AR_CONCEPTS_2026-10-16.md), [ten-page plan](EXO_SPATIAL_STUDIO_TEN_PAGE_IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT_2026-10-16.md), [PPF fit audit](PPF_ACTUAL_GARMENT_FIT_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-10-14.md), [upstream PPF frame forensics](PPF_UPSTREAM_PIN_MOTION_FORENSICS_2026-10-14.md), [fabric library](../integrations/clothcraft/FABRIC_LIBRARY_V2.md), and [EXO fabric optics](EXO_FABRIC_MATERIALS_AND_TRIMS.md).

1. One clothing design, multiple separately verified representations

  1. Authoring source: pattern polygons/curves in measured local coordinates, seam graph, stitch assignments, internal lines, trims, grain direction, measurement spec and graded sizes. Immutable revisions + permitted edits are the source of truth.
  2. Physics input: triangulated rest meshes, masses, local warp/weft orientations, calibrated constitutive material and collision parameters, avatar collider, staging plan, sewing constraints and chosen solver.
  3. Physics output: timed drape frames with the exact source/solver/material hashes, seam/contact/strain QA report, simulation cache status and warning badges.
  4. Presentation: mesh and UVs, appropriate textures, garments on mannequins, shaders, poses, stage/HDRI, camera, video and optimized gallery GLB, all labeled for their actual physical status.
  5. Publishing: a separately authorized visual product page and optionally downloadable pattern/manufacturing data, material assets and studio presets, each with rights and visibility distinct from the original.

A good-looking PBR shader cannot repair an invalid cloth fit. A dense collider does not guarantee reliable seam closure. A published gallery look is not permission to redistribute the original source project.

2. Current implementation audit: what actually works

ClothCraft V2: local native Rust VBD/GPU VBD, three-vertex barycentric Hand spring with continuous target updates, panel selection/movement and material assignment. Its nine native exemplar materials are cotton woven, cotton/poly interlock jersey, wool, denim, nappa leather, light silk, linen, nylon ripstop and stretch knit. The preset mass and some thickness inputs are based on specimens; stretch/shear/bend and look are approximations. The V2 surface generator uses small procedurally repeated bumps and basic MeshPhysicalMaterial properties. There is no experimentally validated complete friction, plasticity, knit-loop or certified textile fit in this pipeline. Relevant files: src/core/clothcraftFabrics.js, clothcraftFabricSurface.js, integrations/clothcraft/exo-session.rs, workspace.mjs and FABRIC_LIBRARY_V2.md.

Broader original Garment Studio: src/core/fabricSurfaceModel.js, textileProcedurals.js, fabricPbrGenerator.js, components/FabricMaterialPreview.jsx and GarmentViewport.jsx provide richer visual texture and environment authoring. Its source model is not automatically the same as the native ClothCraft V2 source model. Migrate through a typed compatibility adapter and comparison fixtures, not by reusing raw numeric labels blindly.

PPF Contact Solver: the installed macOS arm64 Metal runtime has been executed from the EXO PPF bridge. It produced real native cloth trajectories, not a visual fake, but the October 14 historical tee with 98 stitches failed final production fit: mean seam gap near 1.01 cm, maximum about 9.98 cm, cloth/body penetrations beginning frame 3, and swept collision checks failed. A separate near-assembled test failed the required maximum 3 cm initial sewing gap. The upstream 241-frame dress example also had body intersections at frames 87, 90 and 174 under the independent audit. Smoothing a pinned-body keyframe sequence reduced crossings but did not eliminate them. These are hard blockers to calling PPF results certified for fit on this project. See the linked local audit before making a replacement-engine decision.

Official PPF upstream: https://github.com/st-tech/ppf-contact-solver. The Apache-2.0 project expressly describes the solver as an offline/non-production-ready backend and notes slow CPU/Metal performance. Its claim about internal contact safety does not override EXO's separately observed full-trajectory penetration tests. Our goal is to test facts rather than adopt marketing guarantees.

3. The hybrid simulation architecture

Live track: responsive VBD/XPBD

Editor mouse/hand input → raycast to the visible cloth triangle → barycentric weights + bounded target in world meters → native temporary grab → step VBD/GPU VBD → valid drape snapshot → Three.js projection and studio preview. User release, camera loss, app close, mode switch or timeout removes the spring. Maintain revision ownership and a last valid solver state. Do not let an unsupported or unauthenticated website invoke raw local commands.

Offline reference track: PPF

Take a frozen garment source revision and material profile revision. Validate finite geometry, UV/grain topology, 2D-to-3D seam correspondence and initial assembly (no initial disallowed crossings, correct orientation, stitch distances below a chosen gate). Build PPF shell/body groups with explicit pinned-shell vs static policy, thickness and collision settings. Run isolated stages with bounded CPU/GPU quotas and log exact backend/mesh/params. Collect all native time samples and independent tests of intersections, seam closure, stretch, body clearance and swept motion.

States: pending → input validation → staging → native run → full-frame audit → accepted, rejected, failed, cancelled. A solver process exit of 0 means only completed, never automatically accepted. Rejected outputs remain private diagnostics; they cannot silently overwrite an accepted drape, visually approved showroom look or production specification.

Initial research experiments
  • Reproduce the unchanged upstream fitting scene on current hardware with commit and asset hashes.
  • Compare STATIC Thomas vs fully pinned SHELL Thomas using the same starting mesh/sewing/material/gravity/time; one policy variable changed.
  • Fix actual garment 2D→3D armhole, shoulder and side seam correspondence, orientation, physical sewing lengths and nonintersecting initial layout.
  • Independently test every saved frame with triangle intersection and swept CCD checks, including avatar keyframe boundaries; compare native contact diagnostics with independent geometry checks.
  • Benchmark CPU/GPU runtime and memory at multiple triangle counts. Do not extrapolate full interactive speed from a ten-frame 500-vertex test.
  • Measure material parameter convergence separately for jersey, French terry, denim and leather; don't use arbitrary visual presets as physical proof.
Release gate for any PPF-accepted fitting

At minimum require fully enumerated source triangles and stitches; valid material, body, pose and settings; full contiguous frame series and correct frame mapping; zero forbidden body/self penetrations in the tested trajectory; independently checked temporal/swept contacts; bounded maximum/mean seam gaps and regional body clearances; capped nonphysical principal strain; and comparison against actual clothing where users claim true fit. Existing example PPF thresholds from the audit (engineering targets, not certifications) are final seam mean ≤0.2 cm, maximum ≤1 cm, shoulder mean clearance ≤1.5 cm and p90 ≤3 cm, torso mean ≤2 cm. Log failures in red, not merely warning-colored footnotes.

System integration

A future SimulationJob references garmentRevision, triangulationHash, materialProfileVersion, bodyColliderVersion, backendName/Version, runConfigHash, progress, statistics, finalMeshRef, frameCacheRef, acceptanceReport and retention. This fits alongside the creator platform's upload permissions but should not run automatically on public uploads: compute/GPU expenditure and potentially hostile meshes require isolation, quotas and explicit authorization.

4. What a textile profile actually needs

The authoring UI should expose user-friendly physical values but the backend must store canonical units and source measurements.

FieldPhysics meaningCanonical valueInput/calibration note
GSMmass per surface areakg/m² = GSM/1000Weigh known sample area; not garment total mass
Thicknessactual textile compressed/uncompressed thicknessmTest pressure/conditioning matters
Warp extension curvetension/strain along grainload and strain samplesMany fabrics nonlinear and direction-dependent
Weft extension curvecross grain tension/strainload and strain samplesJersey/rib knit differ substantially
Bias/shear responseresistance to rhombus deformationcalibrated force/angleDrape and folds heavily depend on this
Warp/weft bendingdirectional moment/curvaturesolver-model documentedBending should be tested separately from stretching
Damping/hysteresisloading/unloading loss and recoverymodel-specificFabric may remain deformed or relax
Frictioncloth/cloth and cloth/body resistancecontact-model-specificNeed skin, underlying fabric and surface variants
Compression/loftpiled/knit/leather thickness under contactnonlinear measured curveImportant for French terry, fleece, padded garments
Plasticitypermanent shaping/creasesstrain history lawDenim/leather may need irreversible deformation
Environmenthumidity/temp conditioningspecimen metadataCan alter results
Uncertaintyprovenance and confidencesample/testing statusDefault estimates are not scientific certificates

A 220 GSM sample has 0.220 kg/m² nominal sheet mass. At 0.4 m² of cut material the surface-mass-only estimate is 0.088 kg before seam allowances, doubled/folded parts, trims, lining and adhesives. This arithmetic is not fabric hand, stiffness, thread weight or fitted garment mass certification.

Test kit and protocol: controlled-size specimen and scale → conditioned GSM/thickness measurement → repeated warp/weft/bias stretch at more than one load → three-point bending/cantilever strips → friction/sliding against representative body/fabric → drape photo with measured folds → repeat after release and use least-squares/model fitting to infer solver coefficients. Keep raw observation, curve, specimen batch and confidence distinct from fitted backend coefficients.

Important mapping rule: raw user-facing warp/weft stretch in percent at a named force is not automatically interchangeable with PPF normalized Young's modulus, Poisson ratio, thickness and bending parameter; a VBD N/m or N·m coefficient is not numerically the same as a PPF FEM parameter. Create separate calibrated maps for each solver and compare with the same physical fixture. Build backend-specific parameter dictionaries from the same canonical measurement set.

Material priority

Tier 1: cotton jersey, French terry, fleece, heavyweight cotton, canvas, raw and stretch denim, polyester/cotton knit, ribbing and wool suiting. Tier 2: nappa leather, suede, silk/satin, nylon ripstop, fleece/pile, technical shell/coated fabrics, linen, viscose and velvet. Tier 3: multi-layer padded/trapunto, bonded constructions, fusible interfacing, quilting, down/synthetic fill, zippers/hardware with rigid interaction, anisotropic directional trims. Specimen provenance matters more than an impressive count of 300 unmeasured presets.

Suggested MaterialPhysicalProfile fields: materialId, specimenId, description, composition, construction, weaveDirection, surfaceMassKgM2, thicknessM, extensionCurvesByDirection, shearCurve, bendingCurvesByDirection, frictionPairs, damping, compression, permanentSet, uncertainties, environmentalCondition, dataSource, license, validationStatus and derivedBackendCoefficients.

5. Optical appearance is a separate PBR/material problem

A cloth can look like velvet while simulating with a denim preset, so do not assume appearance identifies physics. Every MaterialAsset should link independently to MaterialPhysicalProfile and MaterialVisualProfile.

MaterialVisualProfile: baseColor map/linear color, normalMap, height/displacement, roughness map, ambient occlusion map, weave/nap/loop scale (millimeters/cm), fiber tangent direction, sheen, sheen roughness, anisotropy, clearcoat (only treated/coated surface), thickness/transmission/opacity where relevant, surface front/back, alpha handling, UV scale and source rights. For leather/suede, use grain/nap direction, microrelief and controlled grazing highlights; for denim, repeatable twill angle and dyed warp/weft; for French terry, face knit and terry loop reverse, using separately assigned front/back materials if render backend supports it.

Color pipeline: base-color texture interpreted as sRGB then converted to renderer working color space; scalar roughness/metalness, height and normal are data textures and should not receive sRGB correction. Keep real-world repeat scale and correct directional UV across panel movement. Do not stretch a 3 mm stitch texture to cover a whole sleeve as an arbitrary repeating square. Use texture mipmapping and anisotropic filtering, plus a macro/micro view.

Relevant glTF extensions include KHR_materials_sheen for textile grazing highlights and KHR_materials_anisotropy for directed highlights; renderer and exporter feature support must be checked. Display normal/roughness/albedo maps independently for debugging, and compare shader output under the same lighting, camera, color management, exposure and monitor profile.

Procedural vs measured scans: procedural weave can fill placeholders cheaply; calibrated, tileable captured materials with multiscale normals and reflectance should carry a measured-scan flag. A photo without scale bar, lighting calibration, texture rights and direction annotation is not a physically correct textile source.

Hardware: zipper pullers, metal buttons, buckles and studs need their own real geometry, metallic/dielectric shading and physically meaningful BOM item; they should not simply be embedded in a flat albedo texture. Rigid-body contact for metal trims requires separately validated solver support.

Sample materials gallery tests

A sample scene contains flat swatch macro, bending strip, draped hanging square, hoodie torso and close-up seam at identical calibrated camera/light positions. Repeat for jersey, French terry, denim, leather and suede. Report physics test status beside visual material fidelity; do not silently award a single composite 90% realism score.

6. Photography Studio: build the stage, not a filter

A virtual studio is a scene asset with 3D stage geometry, backdrop/floor, camera rigs, light rigs, HDR environment, world scale, shadow/contact settings, color management, render engine, stage props and asset rights. Keep its original .blend master editable and export runtime derivatives for web. Blender GLB export does not preserve Blender area lights and complete World lighting as losslessly interchangeable scene lighting; EXO web scene recipes must reinstantiate suitable light rigs.

Suggested source scene presets:

  • White Infinity E-commerce: white cyclorama and three-point softboxes, front/back/profile and detail photography camera; same frame crop across products.
  • Neutral Gray Fabric Lab: measured white/gray card, neutral broad illumination plus optional grazing light, swatch spheres, material comparison.
  • Concrete Editorial: original concrete studio, warm key/cool rim, directional floor shadows and fashion still framing.
  • Architectural Gallery: original stone/concrete galleries with columns/vault/tracery and controlled materials, not copied proprietary architectural meshes.
  • Muslin Origins: draped cream muslin backdrop, soft documentary lighting and macro stitch shots.
  • Black Stage: rim-lit garments and hardware, shadow-preserving exposure.
  • Runway Theater: time-controlled dressed avatars, path markers, backstage and audience cameras.
  • Outdoor Light: authorized HDRI and an original ground surface.

Photography panel UX: select Look → Stage thumbnail → Avatar(s) and Pose → Camera/Shot → Lights/Exposure → Render Preview → Queue High-Quality Render → Review/Compare/Re-render → Add to collection or publish. Editing an exposure shouldn't rewrite source textile reflectance; an editorial renderer must not overwrite physically validated neutral swatch captures.

ShotManifest: lookId/version, mannequinIds/poses, garmentRevision/acceptedDrapeCache or visual-only mesh, studioId, camera position/target/lens/crop, lighting rig and HDRI rights, tone map/exposure, resolution, renderEngine/version, outputs and resulting file hashes. Include base PBR visual profile IDs and warn on missing maps.

7. An immersive walk-through 'Wardrobe Museum'

The user described a cool room they can walk through, showing mannequins dressed in all saved clothing. This is a separate view over the same Look and Studio objects, not a second set of duplicate garments.

Spatial UX and room design

Lobby: a 3D architectural hall with collection/season portals, search, favorites, new uploads and 'continue editing'. Collection rooms: runway-like rows of mannequin plinths, each representing a Look; hover on an outfit for floating panel info and thumbnails. Material Room: fabrics, swatches, denim/leather close-up and physical vs visual badges. Pattern Archive: framed technical drawings and 2D panels that open in controlled edit mode; unauthorized viewers can see only permitted redacted previews. Photo Studio Room: an active cyc/set the viewer enters to change cameras/lights and take images. Runway Gallery: a walkable fashion presentation, with saved animation/video and look details. Marketplace doorway: discover permitted public creator looks; no auto-download of their private sources.

Interaction: desktop WASD + mouse (with optional pointer-lock using Three.js PointerLockControls), joystick/gamepad and accessible point-and-click navigation, click-to-teleport, home/reset viewpoint, room mini-map and 'flat accessible browse' alternative. Smooth walking must have collision against walls, floor and mannequins, bounds/navmesh, camera near/far limits and motion-sickness controls. Do not force free-moving first-person on mobile; offer touch orbit and scene hotspot navigation. Render an outline around the chosen mannequin, not heavy emissive fill on the clothing itself.

Privacy: by default only the user's own garments appear. A public showroom is an opt-in published scene with separately licensed textures, mannequins and photos. Team-only rooms respect project ACLs and should not leak hidden pattern measurements via scene files, network responses, embedded GLB extras or developer tools.

Performance and scalability

Stage scene construction in Blender: master .blend → verify licenses, UV, rig, scale and texture dependencies → bake/optimize runtime GLB with LOD variants → compress textures to KTX2/Basis where renderer/device supports → GPU instancing for repeated room props/mannequin plinths → lazy-load rooms, high-resolution fabrics only for nearby outfits, texture memory budgets and recycled cached shaders → preserve a low-performance fallback gallery view.

Provisional performance gate: aim for usable 60 FPS on the chosen current Mac at a specified 1080p window and scene with ~20 mannequins, but benchmark hardware rather than claim this as achieved. Record frame-time median/p95, GPU memory, draw calls, load time and low-detail fallback. Navigation collision should be independent of clothing's full FEM triangles; retain authored garment mapping for inspection but use optimized collision proxies.

No need to simulate every garment live: store accepted drape cache or representative visual look geometry. Full real-time PPF solves while walking an entire room would be a poor use of GPU and is not supported by the current solver.

Example user journey

Open EXO Library → click 'Enter My Room' → walk through the Archive and choose a hoodie mannequin → review front/back, fabric close-up and PPF status → switch colorway → enter its photography studio → render a 3/4 shot → add image to a visual-only creator profile. Selecting 'Open in EXO' returns to the local editable file only when the user is the owner and source is locally available.

8. Engineering phases and acceptance tests

PhaseFuture implementation unitMust prove before proceeding
PH-01Unified physical/visual material schema, provenance and unit adaptersNo 1000× errors; edited material assignment matches known native fixture
PH-02Specimen measurements and backend parameter fitSame swatch curve and drape repeatably map to VBD and PPF separately
PH-03PPF job adapter and failing-fixture regressionHistorical crossings correctly rejected; no accepted-cache overwrite
PH-04Nonintersecting seam assembly and body policy experimentsActual seam and full-frame contact criteria pass on reference garment
PH-05Shared realistic fabric/trim PBR assetsStable texel scale, color space and maps across swatch, garment and scene
PH-06Three Blender studio masters + web runtime derivativesCamera/lighting compare, no missing textures, source .blend preserved
PH-07Walkable single-user wardrobe lobby and Look plinthsVerified first-person navigation, click-to-view, private asset non-leakage
PH-08Multiple collection rooms, accessibility, performance, public room modePermission tests, LOD/budget tests, flat-list fallback, URL sharing only of authorized assets

PPF verification harness should run input validation, staging metrics, fixed/pinned body comparison, all-frame mesh crossing, swept CCD, seam convergence, strain, clearance and disabled acceptance when tests are missing. A screenshot or beautiful final render is not an acceptance fixture.

Materials verification harness should compare fabric specimen/provenance, measurement samples, warp/weft directional response, unit conversions, renderer PBR channels, physical pitch, PPF coefficient mapping and deterministic source-to-render export. Show an 'unmeasured' flag whenever applicable.

Room verification harness should inspect GLB packet for hidden private pattern data, camera navigation collision, load-time RAM/GPU, access control to every asset endpoint, output reproducibility and outside-account denial.

9. Rough dependency/effort bands, not commitments

  • Material schema + unit conversions and tests: 1–3 engineer-weeks.
  • Four core measured textile specimens and calibration pipeline: 4–8+ engineer-weeks plus real specimen/professional testing access.
  • PPF reference solver experimentation: 4–10+ engineer-weeks; significantly more if sewing/contact remains unstable.
  • PBR material library and visual previews: 3–6 engineer-weeks plus look-dev artist time.
  • First three Blender studios and still photo queue: 3–6 engineer-weeks with a Blender artist.
  • Walkable local wardrobe prototype, one room and 10–20 original garments: 3–6 engineer-weeks, plus content preparation. A polished multiroom online experience and multiplayer are larger subsequent programs.

These are order-of-magnitude exploratory bands, not promises or a budget approved for spending. The public platform below should begin with read-only visual previews and static assets before adding remote heavy compute.

10. Source references and rights

  • Original PPF solver (Apache-2.0): https://github.com/st-tech/ppf-contact-solver ; upstream itself warns that Metal/CPU can be slow and solver is experimental.
  • Cloth physics reference (MIT): https://github.com/InteractiveComputerGraphics/PositionBasedDynamics
  • Khronos glTF sheen: https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/blob/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_sheen/README.md
  • Khronos glTF anisotropy: https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/blob/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_anisotropy/README.md
  • Blender Asset Browser: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/5.2/files/asset_libraries/introduction.html
  • Three.js first-person control: https://threejs.org/docs/pages/PointerLockControls.html
  • Local EXO reference: [PPF Metal cloth test](PPF_METAL_CLOTH_VALIDATION_2026-10-13.md), [fabric research](FABRIC_PHYSICS_AND_CLO_RESEARCH_2026-10-16.md), [Garment Studio actual PPF fit audit](PPF_ACTUAL_GARMENT_FIT_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-10-14.md).

Decision: the right architecture is a hybrid physical engine plus independently validated material and photo assets, with a walkable gallery sharing the same Look library. It does not require rewriting EXO V2 or shipping PPF as its real-time Hand backend.
