Scope lock: Nothing in this plan authorizes building or deploying a site, connecting accounts, publishing patterns, charging buyers, uploading work, enabling camera capture, modifying garments, running PPF jobs or altering the installed EXO app. All task IDs below are candidate issues, not filed or assigned issues.

A. Product map and central principle

EXO Studio remains the desktop/native authoring and simulation app. It owns editable garments, original patterns, 2D/3D source, live ClothCraft VBD, optional audited PPF high-fidelity jobs, physical textile test data, procedural/scanned PBR materials, and Blender-created studio presentation. Users retain local ownership of design files.

EXO Community is a separate social and marketplace website. Creators have profiles and private brain-like garment libraries; publish selected Looks as visual-only; optionally grant or sell a specific pattern, garment display mesh, material/PBR texture, manufacturing template, trim, studio scene, avatar or runway asset. Other users follow, curate, download, remix with explicit rights and enter walkable rooms of permitted Looks.

EXO Teams / Tech Packs is a secured workspace on the platform for people developing real garments with other designers, suppliers and manufacturers. The interactive spec includes CAD-linked POM tables, graded sizes, fabrics, trim/BOM, construction and sampling notes, reference photos, pinned comments and immutable manufacturer-specific releases. Factories may propose edits; they cannot silently overwrite a designer's approved source.

Three unequal levels of access must always remain separate:

  1. Can see a public or team-approved sanitized rendered outfit.
  2. Can download particular asset files under exact free or purchased license.
  3. Can edit/approve private source geometry, manufacturing data or a team's working release.

One right never implies the other two. An authorized manufacturer may have full pack data while the creator's followers see only a fashion film or orbitable, reduced 3D view.

Example day-in-the-life

A user opens their private Wardrobe Room, sees all their owned or imported hoodie collections on mannequins, and clicks a garment. The inspector shows fabric weight and measured/estimated reliability, material textures, 2D/3D previews and two simulation badges (interactive preview vs PPF full-frame verified/rejected). From there they can enter a self-authored Concrete Studio, choose a camera/light and render photos. For a chosen garment the Tech Pack shows the approved size/spec, comments from Manufacturer A on sleeve construction and designer responses. Once ready, the user may publish only a visual-only public Look on their profile. A different item may publish a legally licensed free or paid pattern and receive remix citations. The hidden source, client list and factory chat remain private.

B. System decomposition and integration rules

\\\`mermaid

Boundary: the website never mounts the user's entire EXO workspace or raw native localhost Rust commands. EXO Studio initiates a narrow, authenticated, explicit artifact publication job, lists every file to be sent and the permissions, and receives a publish receipt with exact version/hash. A remote viewer cannot start native PPF runs or read local pattern vertices unless a separately authorized future service explicitly offers those operations through reviewed capabilities.

Four catalogs, one identity graph
  • Garment catalog: source garment + exact variants/sizes + editable and display derivatives + fabrication readiness.
  • Materials catalog: physical specimen profile separate from PBR visual texture/profile; each can carry independent licensing.
  • Studio catalog: original editable Blender masters, downloadable studio recipes, optimized web scenes, cameras and lighting rigs.
  • Community catalog: publication packages, author/team, free/paid rights, dependency/remix lineage, gallery rooms, reviews and collections.

Use stable UUIDs with immutable version IDs, origin and content hashes. The same garment source can support 15 different Looks without copying the original. A pattern listing can point to an authorized immutable production package while the creator's private working Tech Pack remains hidden.

C. Technical data contracts worth specifying before code

DomainRequired entity & IDsImmutable versus mutableForbidden assumption
DesignGarment, PatternPiece, Edge, Seam, RevisionRelease immutable; working branch revisioned3D mesh implies editable CAD
PhysicsMaterialPhysicalProfile, SolverRun, ColliderVersion, AcceptanceReportSolver run snapshot + QA immutableSolver completed means safe fit
OpticsMaterialVisualProfile, TextureSet, MapProvenanceVersioned texture source and render profileBeautiful texture implies measured physics
StudioStudioMaster, StudioRuntime, Look, RenderJobMaster protected, published Look frozenBlender GLB retains entire lighting rig
PlatformAccount, Profile, Organization, AssetVersion, PublicationPackagePublished asset version and license receipt immutablePublic preview means source download
CollaborationProjectACL, Comment, Proposal, Revision, FactoryRelease, AcknowledgmentProposal audited; factory release immutableFactory can edit accepted spec directly
MarketplaceOffer, Entitlement, Purchase, Settlement, Refund, LicenseGrantPurchase receipt and delivered version immutablePayment frontend flag grants download

Concept API examples (not active):

  • \POST /v1/publish/preview\ — preview selected files, metadata and rights that *would* be uploaded; no side effect.
  • \POST /v1/publish/commit\ — explicit creator confirmation; upload approved source/derivative with expected version and idempotency key.
  • \GET /v1/assets/{id}/versions/{v}/preview\ — sanitized permitted derivative, never underlying private source.
  • \POST /v1/remixes\ — validate immutable origin, all parent licenses, selected allowed derivative files and creator attribution.
  • \POST /v1/teams/{id}/techpacks/{pack}/proposals\ — propose structured field patch referencing parent release and stable comment anchor.
  • \POST /v1/techpacks/{pack}/releases\ — approver-only, revision-guarded immutable snapshot, generate exact export manifest.
  • \POST /v1/techpacks/{pack}/releases/{release}/acknowledgments\ — factory scoped to received release.
  • \POST /v1/orders/checkout\ and webhook settlement — price/entitlement calculated and verified server-side; no direct client-chosen prices.

Database/caching split: relational database for access, comments, product, asset versions, orders, factory releases and immutable audit; private object storage for source, public CDN for specifically generated display derivatives; explicit safe upload/conversion workers; search index of only public metadata. The local app should not depend on an internet round trip to continue using its existing 2D CAD/simulator.

Capability model for visual-only publishing

The owner selects what is visible: rendered still, orbitable simplified GLB, optional branded studio and selected public fabric descriptions. The publish processor strips source patterns/grades, measurement tables, internal comments, PPF solver coefficients where private, texture EXIF/hidden paths, glTF extras and unpublished other people's IP. Private source files get independent storage keys never transmitted in public responses. Server ACL and signed asset access controls are tested per request. 'Hide Download' UI alone does not secure a file already sent to the browser.

D. Hybrid physics and trustworthy fabric status

Interactive engine: native ClothCraft VBD/GPU VBD supports hand dragging and fast visual response in user workflow. It is appropriate for temporary constraints, stop/reset and room look previews once cached. Its nine native materials are not a completed, lab-calibrated apparel physics library.

High-fidelity contact engine: PPF is a separate asynchronous Rust/Metal batch backend with independently checked trajectory. Its existing EXO run history contains real body crossings and excessive seam gaps; baseline acceptance explicitly failed. Next work must prioritize sound initial sewing assembly, static-vs-pinned body A/B, material conversion and independent full-frame plus swept contact tests. A green 'Fit Verified' badge must not appear on a solver merely finishing with finite output.

Material model: a cloth's physical sheet mass, thickness, warp/weft/bias stretch, shear, bending, friction pairs, hysteresis and compression are a measured specimen or a flagged estimate. Visual albedo, normal, roughness, textile sheen, anisotropy, UV repeat/grain and clearcoat are independent rendering assets. Show both:

  • Physical: \Measured\ / \Calibrated to solver X\ / \Estimated preset\ / \Unknown\.
  • Visual: \Scanned, scale-checked\ / \Procedural\ / \Uploaded, unverified\.
  • Fit: \Visual preview\ / \Solver ran, rejected\ / \Accepted for reference fixture\ / \External garment validation\.

User can select a material under a local hoodie's panels and instantly see woven/knit optics, but to claim credible drape, material coefficients must be fit from the actual sample using known experimental loading, documented solver mappings and same reference garment. Tie source specimen to PBR texture rights only if creator licensed both.

Solving and cost: production site starts with uploaded previews and already cached drape. No anonymous, user-uploaded mesh automatically triggers PPF Metal jobs. Future compute processing needs queuing, per-user quotas, memory/time constraints, cancellation, malware/file type checks and acceptance reports; schedule and charges must be explicitly approved.

E. Virtual wardrobe room, photography and web viewer

Local EXO Studio provides the primary private walkable closet/gallery, with original Blender stages such as White Infinity, Gray Fabric Lab, Concrete Gallery, Architectural Archive, Black Void, Muslin, Runway and Outdoor. The user can navigate WASD/mouse or teleport, choose mannequins and cameras, swap garments, walk into a photography studio and export a local Look. Multiple sizes/colorways and saved avatar poses should feel like an organized fashion collection rather than a filesystem.

Web EXO Community reuses only licensed/sanitized published Look derivatives, optimizes GLB and KTX2 textures, lazy-loads each showroom, restricts private content, and offers accessible 2D galleries. A public room should never reference unapproved private materials/patterns. Users may download or license original scene assets only when that package is explicitly offered separately.

Performance plan: 1 room/10 avatars first, then 20 and several rooms; scene LOD, texture streaming, renderer disposal, shader cache, instanced decor and navigation colliders. Measured FPS and memory on target desktop/mobile matter more than a scene screenshot. Never simulate all outfits in PPF while browsing; use cached drape/visual meshes.

Photography: three original Blender masters before dozens of presets. One neutral e-commerce and one fabric lab for truthful textile checks; a creative Concrete/Gallery set for campaigns. Each Look records garment and asset hashes, avatar rig, pose, lighting, camera, tone mapping and license. A WebGL scene must recreate its own light rig because Blender World and area lights are not faithfully carried by a generic GLB export. Premium offline renders remain a separate local Blender job.

F. Interactive tech packs and manufacturer collaboration as independent product

The web Tech Pack should feel like a living garment specification website, not just an embedded PDF. It has tabbed 2D/3D technical views, size POM tables with method/tolerance, material BOM, cost-restricted sections, construction lines, hardware and zipper references, fit photos, sampling rounds, per-component comments and revision timeline.

The designer owns a Draft branch with private formula, cost and sourcing information. An internal reviewer may annotate actual panel, seam, 3D triangle, measurement row or image. The designer explicitly makes immutable Release R01 with linked EXO source revision and export hashes. Manufacturer A receives only the approved sections/files and can annotate, upload sample measurements/photos, request alternates and acknowledge. A request to change the cuff width becomes a proposal, not a silent edit. The designer creates Draft 02, resolves comments, tests pattern/tech pack, approves Release R02 and the factory sees exactly what changed. The platform remembers R01 as superseded evidence.

Existing EXO source already generates \tech-pack.json\, \factory-spec.html\, \pom-spec.csv\, material/BOM, trims/hardware and DXF export in original Garment Studio; this is a schema/asset source, not an interactive online techpack feature yet. The website should ingest typed metadata, not derive production specs from screenshot OCR. CLO-SET and Techpacker currently provide relevant 3D tech packs, 3D annotations, revisions and factory comments; EXO's differentiator could be linking every item to native pattern/sewing IDs, formally approved immutable manufacturing releases and a community view-only boundary.

No cross-tenant leakage: Manufacturer B may not see A's unit prices, quotes, comments or approved private patterns, even if both collaborate with the same brand. Public followers and marketplace buyers should not receive draft manufacturing notes unless the creator intentionally packages them under a separate license.

G. Creator marketplace and remix network

Public profiles: username/cover/bio, favorite Looks, authored garments, studio rooms, followers/following, free patterns, shop items, licensed remixes, collections and optional verified brand/team account. Private 'My Brain Library' remains separate: drafts, all pattern iterations, measurements, hidden notes, visual references, unfinished design and purchased assets.

Publishing classes: private, unlisted specifically granted, public visual-only, free licensed download, remixable project, paid downloadable asset, team workspace or approved factory release. A listing may have multiple *different* permissioned packages (public still and purchasable DXF; downloadable scene but private original .blend).

Free mass pattern pool: structured size metadata, format validation, author/source license, production-readiness flag, description/preview and download receipts; filters by garment type, style, size, file format and permitted remix/commercial usage. Creators can upload free work without losing ownership. Paid pattern downloads add entitlement, seller onboarding and refund/chargeback handling.

Attribution: every fork tracks exact parent asset revision and rights. Terms distinguish personal use, commercial sewing/production, derivative remixes, source redistribution and promotional use. A studio may contain third-party HDRIs whose original owners do not permit redistributing texture binary files. A contributor royalty graph is a future legally reviewed capability, not something safely inferred merely from asset dependencies.

Social: follow, like, save, comment, remix request, personalized/chronological feed, creator room visit, shared collections, mute/block/report, and moderation. A view-only author can opt out of comments and downloadable derivatives. Keep chronological option for discovery and audit recommendation quality before popularity loops.

Team working: organization roles and per-project ACL, direct garment sharing, invitations/revocations, pinned comments and review queue. A future CRDT may safely merge text/cursor presence, but simultaneous CAD point edits, critical BOM quantities and approved versions need revision guard and conflict review.

Commerce/security: quarantine untrusted uploaded files; never execute arbitrary .blend, model scripts or ZIP payloads on the server unsandboxed. Payments must be verified via provider webhooks, not client flags. Follow seller identity verification, taxes/reporting, platform liability and licensing advice with counsel before launching paid commerce. U.S. copyright and other laws differ by jurisdiction and asset type; don't assume all patterns or designs have identical rights.

H. Program sequence with specific exit gates

P0 — Plan, rights and canonical geometry

Owner: product/EXO architect, security, apparel technologist. Deliverables: sample garments and hashed source fixtures, source-of-truth decision, units, license/consent policy, cloud/compute threat model. Gate: user confirms intended prototype and publishing rights; no existing private project automatically uploaded.

P1 — Local material provenance + PPF research

Owner: native/physics + textiles. Deliverables: unified material measurements/version scheme, typed solver adapters, failing historic PPF fixture, controlled contact A/B test plan, identical-swatch render test. Gate: invalid physical simulation remains rejected, no claimed full fit until tests pass.

P2 — Studio library and first walkable room

Owner: Blender/look-dev, graphics. Deliverables: three original studios, versioned Look metadata, 10 mannequins/looks in private walkable hall, accessible list fallback, material inspector. Gate: original garment hashes unchanged, no licensing violations, target FPS/LOD measured.

P3 — Community core, creator profiles and safe read-only previews

Owner: web/data/security. Deliverables: accounts, profiles, private/public asset boundary, source-safe upload, sanitized preview and creator libraries. Gate: cross-account negative ACL tests and malicious file rejection; published preview has no private CAD/techpack bytes.

P4 — Free downloads, remix licensing and collection graph

Owner: platform/rights. Deliverables: free asset pages, immutable versions, licenses/receipts, search/follows, source dependency graphs and format readout. Gate: unlicensed remix blocked and granted download package hashed.

P5 — Team workflow + read-only interactive Web Tech Pack

Owner: EXO interoperability, web full-stack. Deliverables: garment/POM/BOM/technical view linked to typed native exports, private factory role and annotated comments. Gate: Manufacturer A cannot see B/private fields; measurement units and size variants match known EXO fixture.

P6 — Draft/release workflow + sampling

Owner: apparel specialist, backend/security. Deliverables: structured change requests, sample photos/measurements, version diff, approver-signed R01/R02 and acknowledgment, historical export hashes. Gate: released specs immutable and diff reproducible, access revocable.

P7 — Paid marketplace and seller operations

Owner: commerce/legal/security. Deliverables: seller accounts, payment provider integration, order entitlements, refunds, payout/tax controls, moderation and support operations. Gate: unauthorized download denied, chargeback handled according to defined rights, financial audit trail retained.

P8 — Public creator rooms and advanced collaboration

Owner: realtime/graphics/privacy. Deliverables: public walkable creator showcases with only licensed derivatives, optional shared view/presence, optimized 3D rooms and accessibility. Gate: room performance, permissions and moderation at real expected traffic.

P9 — Optional advanced PPF, royalties and AR

Owner: research/physics/commercial. Deliverables: validated PPF high-quality processing only after physical contact acceptance, legally reviewed multi-author revenue shares, separately scoped AR try-on/fit research. Gate: never present unvalidated output or noncommercial research model as factory-grade or unrestricted commercial product.

Prioritization: working slices, not dependency lock-in

Do not require PPF to pass to launch a local visual studio. Do not require the paid store to provide a free community pool. Do not require expensive 3D rooms before simple creator galleries and download pages. Tech Pack collaboration may ship for a private small brand/workshop before a public social launch, if permissions/version contracts are ready. Keep authorization for these as separate decisions.

EXO Web Tech Pack — Living Product Specification, Revisions & Factory Collaboration

Future concept research and implementation plan | 16 October 2026 | No build authorized

Program navigation: [Integrated ecosystem master architecture](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRAM_2026-10-16.md), [shared platform/physics/material candidate backlog](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_CANDIDATE_ENGINEERING_BACKLOG_2026-10-16.md) and [community creator marketplace](EXO_COMMUNITY_MARKETPLACE_SOCIAL_COLLABORATION_ARCHITECTURE_2026-10-16.md).

Vision: transform the static 2D paper/PDF tech pack into a secure interactive garment product website. A designer and manufacturer inspect an approved 2D/3D garment, measurements, size grading, trims, fabrics, construction instructions, reference images and sample review threads in one linked workspace. Each update is documented, compared, accepted or rejected, and attributed to the correct person. The original factory export remains available as a versioned fallback, but the living web specification becomes the collaborative source for future product development.

Cross-references: [EXO marketplace and collaboration architecture](EXO_COMMUNITY_MARKETPLACE_SOCIAL_COLLABORATION_ARCHITECTURE_2026-10-16.md), [EXO physical/visual garment research](EXO_PPF_MATERIALS_IMMERSIVE_STUDIOS_FUTURE_ARCHITECTURE_2026-10-16.md), [EXO production pipeline](EXO_PRODUCTION_PIPELINE.md), [technical pattern/manufacturing parity plan](GARMENT_STUDIO_COMPLETE_CLO3D_PARITY_MASTER_PLAN_2026-10-13.md).

1. Target experiences and views

Designer Workspace: full editable product source, technical drawings, formulas, grading, fabrics and suppliers, costs, confidential sample notes, design inspirations, internal targets, restricted assets, manufacturing release tools, private team tasks and proposal merges.

Manufacturer Workspace: the specific approved release and selected fields explicitly shared with this factory: measurement/size charts and tolerances, pattern package when authorized, construction callouts, BOM material identifiers, approved colors, trim placement, QA expectations, reference photos and sample comments. Manufacturer can flag manufacturing concerns, attach production photos, propose alternate stitch/materials and request clarification. Changes are proposed, not immediately authoritative.

Public/Portfolio View: only a separately published visual outfit presentation and any intentionally public facts. No internal POM rows, proprietary measurement formulas, construction recipes, raw pattern coordinates, vendor costs or factory messages should be sent to browser clients from this view.

Buyer/License View: if a designer sells a downloadable garment pattern or tech pack, show only the purchased licensed package/version; a marketplace purchase does not automatically grant access to the internal collaborative factory workflow.

User flow: hoodie sample review
  1. Designer creates a hoodie in EXO Studio, links fit avatar, fabric profile, variant/size run and a visual garment preview.
  2. Designer opens Web Tech Pack > Draft 04, adds chest/waist/sleeve length tables, tolerance notes, grading, construction stitches, padded-hood details, hardware BOM and reference images.
  3. Internal patternmaker and brand lead review with annotations pinned to 2D panel edges and 3D shoulder/hood geometry.
  4. Designer creates immutable Factory Release R01 from a specified garment source revision, POM, BOM and construction snapshot. Release shows creation time, author, status and full hash.
  5. Assigned Manufacturer A receives a secure project-scoped link to R01, can see permitted drawings and download only approved pattern/export attachments.
  6. Factory attaches a photo of Sample 1 and comments on the sleeve cuff row: “The opening is 1.2 cm too wide relative to R01 tolerance.” Designer gets an assigned issue linked to the specific size and POM ID.
  7. Designer edits a new Draft 05, adjusts the point of measure, updates tech notes, checks the pattern source and asks for review.
  8. Diff screen compares R01 and proposed R02: updated cuff dimensions across sizes, fabric and stitch spec unchanged, revised reference photo added, unresolved comments flagged.
  9. Approved R02 is issued; Manufacturer A must acknowledge receipt. R01 remains accessible as superseded evidence rather than silently changing.
  10. Subsequent Sample 2 feedback attaches to R02, not to an undefined 'latest' floating version.

Result: creators and factories can reconstruct exactly what was approved, when, by whom, with which garment/measurement files. A screenshot and PDF of each release can be generated for offline production.

2. Browser layout and content panels

Proposed route /teams/{team}/styles/{style}/techpacks/{pack}/revisions/{revision}; public routes never mirror this private path unless specifically approved.

Top: style name, development season, SKU, colorway, current status (Draft / In Review / Changes Requested / Approved / Released / Superseded / Archived), active source document and release version, factory partner, language and updated timestamp.

Left contents: Overview, Moodboard/References, Technical Flat, 3D Garment, POM & Sizes, Grade Rules, Fabric/Material, Trims/Hardware, Sewing & Construction, Colorways/Artwork, Labels & Packaging, BOM & Costing, Sampling, QC/Approvals, Files, Revision History.

Center: selectable 2D flat diagrams and 3D garment with annotations. Manufacturer can click zipper, seam, printed graphic or edge and read only permitted technical callouts. Images can be zoomed, layered and commented upon. Side-by-side visual for original vs revised garment at matched camera and pose. Simple mobile read-only layout with anchored comment cards when canvas is too small.

Right: properties/measurements and contextual comments, task assignee, linked sample photos, approval state, discussion history, change proposals, mentions and files. Pinned comments should have resolved/open filters and mention notification controls.

Footer / sidebar: status changes, upcoming sampling dates, references to approved PDF/export ZIP and audit history; keyboard-accessible comment navigation.

Version comparison: per-field colored diff at category level, including centimeters/millimeters with tolerance, size chart modifications, color/material changes, added/removed pattern pieces, stitch/fastener changes, images, and approval notes. Some 3D geometry differences cannot be represented as simple numeric values; provide 2D contour/3D ghost overlays in matching coordinate frames plus a changed-panel list.

3. Technical source data reused from EXO

Existing EXO already generates useful manufacturing artifacts: src/core/measurements.js builds a structured Tech Pack object; src/core/factoryPackage.js generates tech-pack.json, pom-spec.csv, cost/factory data and export ZIP; native_cpp/core/tech_pack_export.cpp and native_cpp/core/factory_package_files.cpp offer a parallel native path including factory-spec.html, materials-bom.csv, construction-seams.csv and trims-hardware.json. These are existing export paths, not evidence of an operational collaborative Tech Pack website.

The future web schema should import stable EXO IDs and original source revision; it should not parse PDF text to recover manufacturing facts. Accept a semantic schema version, SI canonical measures, display cm/mm/in conversions, sizes and grading metadata, and links to visual read-only source previews. If some EXO fields are missing, show not supplied rather than fabricating a value.

Version data types (proposal)
  • TechPack: packId, garmentId, teamId, ownerId, currentWorkingDraft, releasedVersionIds, factoryScopes, productStatus.
  • TechPackRevision: revisionId, parentRevisionId, sourceGarmentRevision, contentHash, actor, createdAt, status, schemaVersion, reviewPolicy, fieldChangeSet.
  • SectionSnapshot: refImage entries, drawings, POM table, fabric/profile references, BOM, trims, artwork, grading, construction, labeling, packaging, qcNotes, supplier-facing instructions.
  • SpecLine: stable lineId, category, pointOfMeasure, description, sizeDimensionValues, baseSize, toleranceUpper/Lower, methodOfMeasurement, units, validationStatus.
  • Attachment: fileId, source type, uploader, version, access classification, malware scan state, external provenance/license, linked entity ID.
  • Sample: factory, sampleRound, size/colorway, shipped/received dates, measurement report, photo set, problems and resolution state.
  • Proposal: proposed patch with expectedRevision and target IDs, factory notes, priority, affected variants, evidence and review decision.
  • Release: immutable snapshot hash, approver IDs and time, approved buyer/factory audience, signature/acknowledgment, supersedes, export manifest.
  • Comment: threadId, stable anchor object, author, mentions, createdAt, visibility scope, status, assignedTo, edit history and evidence.

Sample state graph: Draft → Internal Review → Approved → Released; Released may become Superseded only when another approved release is issued. Rejected proposals and unresolved comments remain historical records. Edits to a released version must never occur in-place; create a new draft based on its snapshot.

4. Designer vs manufacturer view: security is mandatory

Access is controlled server-side per team, project, release, actor role and exact file, not just CSS tabs. Designer can see internal cost assumptions and factory quotes separately; Manufacturer A sees only their explicitly approved release and related discussion, not Manufacturer B's pricing, drafts or internal creative comments. A guest link may be time-limited and restricted to a specific released revision; “unlisted” alone is not sufficiently safe.

Review roles: Owner / Designer / Patternmaker / Approver / Manufacturer / QC / Guest. Permissions include view_public_preview, read_private_working_draft, modify_design_spec, propose_factory_change, comment, upload_sample_evidence, approve_internal, release_to_factory, factory_acknowledge, download_pattern_package and access_costs. Assign grants at project and release scope, and explicitly deny grants by default.

Private mode: a creator can still publish the outfit visually to the public social site, while hiding full measurements, pattern geometry, PPF parameters and the whole tech pack. This requires a sanitized visual-only package and no unapproved technical metadata in the GLB, network response or public image alt text.

5. Comments tied to stable 2D and 3D anchors

2D anchor: reference garment sourceVersion, panelId, curveId/edgeId/pointId, local (u,v)/distance along edge plus fallback world-space XY in canonical units, visibility, and viewport transform version. Best anchor is a stable logical edge or measurement ID rather than raw pixel location.

3D anchor: mesh asset version, triangle ID + barycentric weights + optional surface normal and garment panel ID. A physically simulated mesh can be remeshed/reindexed: prefer logical source panel/feature identifier; if remapping fails, mark anchor needs relinking and keep the original screenshot so the remark does not jump to an unrelated vertex.

Measurement anchor: pointOfMeasure stable ID + size/colorway and field path. BOM anchor: line item ID and vendor/material row. Image anchor: stable image ID and normalized [0..1] XY. Workflow anchor: sampling stage, date, release version or request ID.

Each thread should support reply, mention, assign, resolve/reopen, attachment, notify participants and immutable edit/audit records. A factory cannot rewrite earlier acknowledged statements or approved specs without an audit trail. A comment edit may be allowed with separate original and new text records under retention rules.

Collaboration technology choice

Yjs/CRDT can handle concurrent rich-text notes and ephemeral presence, but measurement tables, BOM quantities, source pattern geometry and release approval must use validated server transactions and expectedRevision locking. Two people entering different numbers for the same cuff width cannot safely “auto-merge” into a new production value. Show a conflict with both proposals, not silent last-write-wins. Offline comments sync only after server ACL and released revision are revalidated.

6. Data correctness: BOM, size runs and reference images

POM changes need cross-size constraints, e.g. allowed tolerance by size and method of measurement, not simply free-form paragraphs. The draft editor shows cm/in preview conversion but stores one canonical unit. Compare original design measurements with *actual pattern or 3D derived measured values* only if those measurements are robustly extracted. Display separate columns for target spec, tested sample actual, deviation and approve/rework decision.

BOM must preserve supplier, material ID, supplier SKU, composition, estimated usage/quantity by garment/size, unit price, effective date, colorway restrictions and substitutes. Public garment pages never reveal confidential supplier/unit costs unless approved. Production export source XML/CSV/JSON fields must correspond to displayed release, with hashes to prevent ambiguity.

Reference imagery is not merely a moodboard decoration. Store target detail (e.g. cuff shape, topstitch, lining, wash outcome), uploader, rights, size-specific scope, review status, and linkage to the relevant panel, POM, BOM or construction line. Present images alongside the actual accepted spec; a factory sees whether an image is “inspiration,” “illustrative non-binding” or “approved manufacturing reference.”

A Technical Flat is distinct from a beauty render. It must show authorized seam construction, grain/curve references and technical dimensions, not claim production correctness based on the photo scene. An external downloaded pattern's license may permit viewing but not factory mass production; attach license terms to each approved package.

7. Manufacturing workflow and sampling iteration

Dev Stage: concept → first pattern → proto sample → fit sample → size set → preproduction sample → production release → shipment QC. These are configurable labels per manufacturer; the product must not invent a globally mandatory supply-chain process.

At each sample round the factory can upload sample photos, POM actuals, workmanship findings, production feasibility, material alternates and shipping evidence. The designer reviews deviations and either accepts-with-reason, requests rework, or drafts a formal spec change. A sample approval must name size/colorway, fabric lot where relevant, inspector and exact release hash. It cannot blanket-approve later unreviewed size changes.

Change Request records target revision, rationale, proposed field diff, affected BOM/POM/pattern list, cost/schedule impact estimate, manufacturer discussion and final decision. Approved requests create or update a new draft; the designer publishes a new immutable release after review. Manufacturers acknowledge R01→R02 and the site shows that R01 is superseded. For factories offline, distribute a release-tagged PDF/ZIP with a QR/link/reference ID; that export never becomes a mutable “latest.pdf” with no snapshot.

Proposed sequencing: TP-001–003 (read-only spec page) → TP-004–007 (working review room) → TP-008–010 (factory-controlled release) → TP-011–014 (safe notifications/public integration and reliability). Do not start public factory invitation until the ACL and immutable snapshot engine passes repeated tests.

Qualitative staffing and effort

Dedicated backend/security, EXO CAD schema expert, UI/3D specialist and production-apparel reviewer. A basic read-only Tech Pack linked to an existing design may be a several-week prototype; manufacturer-grade permissions, concurrent reviews, versioned releases, audit and robust exported factory files are a multi-month independent product effort, dependent on schema and review cycles. No approved delivery date or spend is implied.

9. Proposed web screen sketches (textual)

Designer: header "HOODIE 001 · Draft 05 · Awaiting Pattern Review"; subnav "3D / 2D / Measurements / Fabrics / Construction / Samples / Versions"; left technical drawing; right open issue "Cuff POM #C17 Sample1 +1.2cm" assigned to designer; inline side-by-side R01→Draft05, private cost table and button "Request approval" (not automatically release).

Manufacturer: header "HOODIE 001 · Approved R01 · Acknowledge Revision"; center construction drawing and approved size chart; right sections "Factory Questions / Upload Sample / Propose Change"; R02 notice only after approved; no source formula edit controls, private design notes, or internal cost fields.

Public: header "HOODIE 001 by Creator"; 3D orbit, stage photo, fabric description and allowed public notes; optional Buy/Download Pattern if separately licensed. No techpack version/internal notes or factory assets transmitted.

10. Prior art: what existing products prove

Techpacker publicly advertises digital card-based tech packs, real-time comments, teamwork and version comparison. Its help pages document versioned sharing to manufacturers and note read-only factory functions including commenting and attaching files. Backbone documents product feedback revisions and pulling fit/sample reports into tech packs. This validates specific feature categories, not a claim that all competitors have the exact EXO 3D-native data/approval model.

EXO should differentiate by a first-class native garment-to-TechPack link (stable 2D/3D garment IDs), comment anchoring in the actual cloth scene, exact version/size diff, independently approved factory snapshots, 3D rooms and creator-public storefront privacy separation.

Additional directly relevant CLO-SET benchmark (verified 2026-10-16): CLO-SET's Web Tech Pack Construction documentation describes an online tech pack populated from a garment's 3D content, data synchronized from CLO3D BOM, editable construction markers and annotation of materials, trims and sewing methods. It notes that the Construction pane's support differs between its legacy and newer content-page views; do not infer all accounts/products expose the same menu. Its 2025 annotation update describes selecting pattern, fabric or trim components in a 3D Tech Pack pane and creating linked 3D annotations. This proves the 3D data-linked approach is commercially relevant, not that EXO can copy CLO proprietary source or inherits CLO cloud features automatically.

Differentiation gates for EXO: (1) the browser never uses PDF extraction as the authoritative POM/BOM source; (2) comment anchors carry stable EXO pattern feature IDs and a mesh revision rather than only pixels; (3) factories read exact approved release Rnn and must acknowledge changes; (4) the system can publish a visual-only social Look without delivering private techpack/manufacturing records; (5) permitted downloads and pattern licensing are separately recorded from manufacturer collaboration grants. Competitors may already provide portions of these; validate actual release/version before making comparison claims.

Research:

  • Techpacker current feature overview: https://techpacker.io/
  • Techpacker secure factory sharing and versions: https://helpcenter.techpacker.com/hc/en-us/articles/360022720553-How-to-share-a-Tech-pack-with-Manufacturers
  • Techpacker comments: https://helpcenter.techpacker.com/hc/en-us/articles/360017617214-How-to-communicate-with-team-members-and-manufacturers
  • Techpacker factory role behavior: https://documentation.techpacker.com/techpack/communicate/share.html
  • Backbone product revisions: https://intercom.help/backbone-help-center/en/articles/5803263-product-feedback-revisions
  • Yjs shared text for concurrent notes: https://docs.yjs.dev/getting-started/working-with-shared-types
  • CLO-SET Web Tech Pack Construction and BOM integration: https://support.clo-set.com/hc/en-us/articles/45303219780377-Web-Tech-Pack-Construction
  • CLO-SET Annotation and 3D Tech Pack: https://support.clo-set.com/hc/en-us/articles/45303128139673--2025-04-Updates-in-CLO-SET-Annotation-work-with-3D-Tech-Pack

Decision: Treat the Web Tech Pack as a collaborative, permissioned product development room with machine-readable source data, immutable release approvals and linked manufacturing evidence; static PDF becomes one export of a frozen version, not the sole collaboration interface. Nothing is built or published by this document.

EXO Community — Profiles, Garment Pattern Pool, Creator Marketplace & Collaboration

Product + platform architecture research | 2026-10-16 | Concept only, no implementation

User experience extension: [Premium web Community, in-editor Pattern Lab, and sample Web Tech Pack design system](EXO_COMMUNITY_PATTERN_LAB_PREMIUM_UI_UX_MASTER_2026-10-16.md). Isolated clickable prototype: [Website and Pattern Lab](../concepts/exo-community-ui/index.html). Demo-only rights and import states; no production service is enabled.

Program navigation: [Integrated ecosystem implementation program](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRAM_2026-10-16.md) and [creator marketplace / team candidate engineering issues](EXO_ECOSYSTEM_CANDIDATE_ENGINEERING_BACKLOG_2026-10-16.md).

Product direction. Build an independent consumer-facing EXO Community website, connected to EXO Studio via explicit user-controlled publish and download workflows. The site is a Sketchfab-like fashion network: people have maker profiles, outfits, patterns, fabrics, sewing assets, rigged avatars, Blender studios and fashion show environments. Others can browse a huge free pool, download legally allowed assets, pay for others, remix with correct attribution, collaborate with teams, follow designers and visit walkable virtual fashion spaces.

Protect creator agency: Public visibility of a beautiful garment is not consent to share editable CAD patterns, grading rules, a manufacturing tech pack, raw measured fabric data, private team discussions or full-resolution meshes. Uploaded source, presentation derivatives, approved downloads, team-only working versions and public profile entries must be different storage/security concepts.

Related: [EXO PPF/materials/rooms research](EXO_PPF_MATERIALS_IMMERSIVE_STUDIOS_FUTURE_ARCHITECTURE_2026-10-16.md), [spatial blueprint](EXO_SPATIAL_STUDIO_TEN_PAGE_IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT_2026-10-16.md), [execution backlog](EXO_SPATIAL_STUDIO_EXECUTION_BACKLOG_2026-10-16.md), [interactive web tech pack future](EXO_INTERACTIVE_WEB_TECHPACK_PLANNING_2026-10-16.md).

1. Two products, one creator identity

EXO Studio is the local native/browser garment editor: editable 2D patterns, dimensions, native/PPF simulations, material authoring, 3D avatars, local pattern manufacturing export, staged photography and immersive wardrobe. Production data stays local until expressly uploaded or connected.

EXO Community is the website: public/private creator pages, a 3D viewer, free pattern pool, commercial marketplace, remix tree, likes/follows/collections, creator rooms and collaborative team workrooms. It only receives artifacts the creator chooses to publish or share, never blanket access to local disk.

EXO Teams / Product Development adds organization projects, separate permissions, comments, review/approval and interactive web tech packs. A brand may invite manufacturers to review an approved specification without offering that proprietary source to random followers.

Suggested website navigation:

  • Discover: personalized but controllable chronological/search feed, categories, latest public garment looks, pattern pool and trending studios.
  • Patterns: free/open and paid CAD/pattern listings with formats, sizes, number of pieces, seam-allowance status and compatibility notes.
  • Marketplace: Garments, Patterns, Fabrics/Materials, Trims/Hardware, Avatars, Studios/Scenes, Runways, Collections, Tools/Templates.
  • Profiles: creator's bio, collections, visual garment wall, followers/following, remix credits, stores, work published, external links and badges.
  • Rooms: immersive private/public outfit museums from verified published Look assets.
  • Teams: workspaces, review queues, assigned tasks, secure files, comments and live Tech Packs.
  • My Library: owned assets, downloaded licenses, saved patterns, purchased items, remixes and subscriptions.
  • Upload/Publish: guided wizard with private content selection, license, copyright, preview, scan and final publish confirmation.

2. Models of publication: never treat public as downloadable

A product/creator entry can expose different packages:

Publish intentPublic can seePublic receivesOwnership constraint
Private projectNothingNo network responseOwner/team only
Unlisted proof/reviewSelected preview only, gated linkNo download unless grantedToken scoped and revocable
Public visual portfolioThumbnails, filtered 3D display, permitted notesNo editable pattern/mesh sourceViewer-only derivative with no confidential metadata
Free patternPreview + selected distributable pattern filesZIP/DXF/SVG/AAMA only if legitimately exported and documentedExplicit free license and creator permission
Open remix projectView + specified source, version and dependenciesSource files under clear permission to forkPreserve upstream credits, derivative terms
Paid digital productTeaser and license tiersEntitled signed download after confirmed transactionSeller rights and platform settlement
Team projectRole-filtered metadata, previews and annotationsFiles based on project role and explicit grantOrganization/workroom ACL
Factory tech packApproved snapshot and revisionsFactory-scoped approved specifications and authorized packagesManufacturer cannot silently edit designer's master

View-only is not perfect DRM: users may screenshot visible artwork or reverse-engineer some approximate geometry. Security means never transmit restricted source bytes, not simply hiding a download button. Generate genuinely reduced/rebaked display derivatives that omit proprietary 2D coordinates, formulas, grade rules, source filenames, metadata, author comments and precise manufacturing seam labels. Scrubbing glTF extras and metadata is necessary but not sufficient; inspect network payloads. Even an accurate 3D view may expose commercially relevant visual design, which the creator accepts when publishing.

3. Rich catalog taxonomy and universal asset records

AssetType: garment pattern, garment display/look, fabric physical profile, fabric visual/PBR scans, hardware trim, graphic/print, embroidery/seam template, avatar/pose/rig, studio environment, runway set, collection/lookbook, manufacturing template and tutorial.

Asset record: assetId UUID, type, creator/team owner, title, slug, summary, searchable tags, garment category, applicable sizes/region standards, software/format compatibility, asset source reference, rights and license, base price and currency, preview references, thumbnails, approved downloadable package refs, dependency IDs/versions, content hash, publishing state, editability badge, external source provenance, maturity/validation status and moderation status.

Pattern specifics: paper/reference pattern vs verified production-grade document, dimension units, grading coverage, seam allowance type, notches, drill marks, grainline, internal construction lines, number of pieces, fabric width/layout advice, optional PDF/SVG/DXF/other legally interoperable formats. Public listing must not claim factory-ready accuracy from unverified SVG screenshots.

Fabric specifics: sample source, measured GSM and thickness, optional loading curves/solver coefficients, physical calibration status, weave/knit description, UV repeat scale, separately licensed texture maps, author and colorway. A material can be downloaded as visual-only (PBR textures) without giving the commercial lab measurement set, or vice versa where terms allow.

Studio specifics: source .blend allowed only when creator authorizes, portable GLB scene when compatible, original HDRIs and props with their own rights, cameras/lights, environment preview, performance budgets and scene license. A studio's purchased license does not magically include permission to redistribute bundled third-party material packs.

Derivatives: each derived publication links sourceAssetId and exact sourceRevision, the base material/trim/studio dependency manifest, declared transformations, original creator credit, evidence of license compatibility and a graph of upstream distribution terms.

Creator profile layout

Cover image and username; studio alias/handle; public biography and optional verified business profile; featured outfits in 3D, public room entrance, Pattern Pool submissions, fabrics/studios tabs, followers/following, saved collections, remix lineage and seller store. A private dashboard holds draft and team projects, unshared studies and paid sales reports, never visible merely because the public visits the profile.

Suggested creator types: independent designer, clothing brand, patternmaker, textile artist, 3D artist, factory account and educator. No occupation claim is verified until the platform has a responsible verification process.

4. Remix graph and licenses are the product, not an afterthought

Creators want to build on a shared sweatshirt base, modify panels and publish a new hoodie or reuse an original fabric/studio. A naive download button will break attribution and potentially infringe rights.

Core principles: the upload author must attest that they hold redistribution and derivative rights; remixable requires an explicit license that permits modification; paid/downloadable is *not* automatically remixable or resellable; each derived item records exact upstream versions and original authors. Store a license receipt at each download and purchase.

User-facing options: visual display only; free personal use; free with attribution; open commercial remix under a specified compatible license; paid personal/commercial usage with optional redistribution rights; separate extended physical-production usage. Do not invent ambiguous 'free to use however you want' permissions. Creative Commons licenses can cover some art assets; source code uses software licenses; garment patterns and manufacturing terms may need carefully drafted industry-specific license terms and local legal review.

Composite remix licensing: a derivative garment may incorporate an original base pattern, a paid denim visual material, a button asset and a licensed runway stage. Its public metadata can credit all contributors, but the right to package or resell component files depends on every original license. Some assets are *reference in project* rather than redistributable. Automatically show conflicts and block a publication containing incompatible rights.

Royalty split (long-term optional): map dependencies to an entitlement graph, choose explicit percentages and order-of-operations for each paid sale, cap complexity/cycles, handle refunds/chargebacks, and store settlement evidence. This is a commercial/legal project of its own, not something a runtime asset hash alone can solve. CLO-SET CONNECT's design-source policy offers a concrete reference for distributing proceeds to component creators; do not simply copy its fee schedule or assume the same terms work for EXO.

Marketplace economics research

CLO-SET CONNECT already lists Garment, Fabric, Trim, Avatar and Scene categories with free/paid publishing and basic/extended licensing. Its help center currently says it charges 30% commission; this is a competitive observation, not a recommended EXO fee. Sketchfab demonstrates 3D annotations, profiles, embedding and private/unlisted controls. Differentiate EXO by genuine pattern source/size metadata, author-approved remix rights, material mechanics, interactive factory tech packs and local EXO app round trips.

Stripe Connect is a candidate for seller onboarding, marketplace settlements, payouts, refunds and fee splitting. Its pricing, tax collection roles, supported geographies and liability must be reviewed when implementing; do not hardcode a nonexistent fee or claim it handles every seller's tax obligations automatically. Paid sales should require a real entitlements state and signed short-lived download, not a frontend purchase flag.

5. Collaboration: independent editing, review, and permissions

Team workspace: organization brand, teams, project boards, roles, shared asset registry, milestones, assigned tasks, review notes, garment version history and manufacturing partners. Invite by verified user/account and explicit project scope, with invitation expiry/revocation.

Proposed roles (least privilege):

  • Owner: invite/revoke admins, modify rights, publish and initiate sales.
  • Admin/Manager: manage team work and assign reviews; cannot transfer ownership without owner approval.
  • Designer/Patternmaker: edit authorized garment sources/measurements and propose a revision.
  • Material specialist: edit approved fabric/trim records, not unrestricted all-project assets.
  • Reviewer: annotate and approve only when designated; no general edit.
  • Manufacturer: read approved release snapshot, propose questions/change requests and attach sample evidence; cannot silently overwrite canonical spec.
  • Client/Guest: restricted preview/feedback, expiration and watermark optional.
  • Public user: only sanctioned released files and public derivative.

Real-time collaboration: rich-text descriptions, comments, checklists and annotations can use a CRDT such as Yjs for concurrent text; structured garment CAD editing should initially use transaction locks/optimistic revisions, deterministic operations and conflict resolution, not merge arbitrary point edits by last-write-wins. An edit to the same curve control point at two devices needs a visible conflict rather than hidden data loss. Live viewers may follow a coworker's camera, inspect a diff or comment anchored to an object stable ID.

Branching and approvals: Draft branch → internal review → technical/pattern review → manufacturer request → approved immutable Release, with ability to fork a new draft. One release version is always identifiable for a purchase/factory run; a previous approval must not be implicitly inherited after measurement or material changes. Events have actor, clock time, old/new revision, project/garment, before/after data paths and reason.

Comments: anchor to pattern panel ID and point/edge stable ID, 3D triangle barycentric surface with mesh revision, BOM line, size/measurement row, image pixel normalized coordinates or document page/section. When geometry changes, try remap anchor with known stable IDs; otherwise mark unresolved / orphaned rather than silently pinning to an unrelated point. Thread, mentions, resolution, assigned owner, due date and evidence image belong to the comment record. See the [web tech pack plan](EXO_INTERACTIVE_WEB_TECHPACK_PLANNING_2026-10-16.md).

6. Web architecture and systems

Frontend: separate EXO Community app (e.g., modern React framework, browser 3D renderer built atop Three.js); public SEO pages, profile and catalog. The existing EXO desktop app should integrate only through a user-controlled publisher/importer with scoped account authentication, not by opening local ports to the internet.

Backend: authenticated API, relational database for profiles/permissions/assets/orders/version metadata, private blob/object storage for originals, derivative/CDN storage for public preview, entitlement service for purchased assets, search index with optional semantic tags, background job queue for thumbnails/virus scans/conversions. Organization/project isolation enforced on server, not just hidden frontend tabs. Audit logs are append-only from the application perspective.

File pipeline: signed upload destination → quarantine → MIME/magic/type scan and strict allowed formats → decompression bomb / path traversal / executable-content checks → size, file count, triangle, texture and archive limits → provenance/license review → safe source storage → sandboxed parser/render conversion → public derivative preview → user approves exact publish bundle → CDN. Never directly execute code embedded in a model asset or Blender .blend; studio scene processing is a sandboxed offline job. Follow the OWASP upload guidance.

Private boundaries: raw original patterns, manufacturing specs, personally identifying account data, factory notes and all purchased sources live in private storage. Every download checks entitlement and exact asset version at request time. Public search only indexes public sanitized metadata. A profile viewer should never receive hidden measurements or private object-store URLs. Avoid 'unlisted' as the sole protection for confidential factory specs.

Conceptual normalized database entities

users / profiles / organizations / org_members / teams / projects / project_members / garments / source_revisions / assets / asset_versions / asset_files / licenses / entitlements / imports / derivative_edges / collections / collection_items / follows / reactions / comments / threads / moderation_reports / studio_rooms / looks / reviews / releases / manufacturer_partners / orders / order_items / payment_events / settlements / notifications / immutable_audit_events.

Relational invariants: asset_versions immutable when published; orders reference stable offered asset version/license price at purchase time; derivation graph is acyclic (unless explicitly represented as non-derivative reference); creator can remove future sales while retaining necessary proof for prior buyers under policy; hard deletion/erasure honors privacy law and retained transaction obligations. Sales or asset counts on a profile are derived from authorized queries, not editable arbitrary fields.

Example asset manifest (illustrative only)

asset: "uuid"

type: "garment-pattern"

owner: "creator-uuid"

edition: "2026.1"

visibility: "public-preview-only"

previews: ["approved-preview-id"]

sourceFiles: ["private-source-object-id"]

downloads: []

grants: [{ principal: "team", role: "viewer" }]

sourceDependencies: []

rights: { display: true, download: false, remix: false }

provenance: { uploader: "...", rightsAttested: true }

validation: { unitsChecked: false, productionReady: false }

This is a schema sketch, not a functioning security policy or final licence legal text. All grants require backend verification; a public JSON snippet should never expose private source object IDs to a different account.

7. Trust, moderation, creator protection and community safety

Large public libraries require active safety design: copyrighted works, counterfeit brand logos, misleading 'certified fit' listings, spyware disguised as downloadable tools, plagiarized patterns, unauthorized human scans/photos, explicit or harmful imagery, harassment in comments and spam. Design reporting, appeal and action workflows, a repeat-infringer/copyright notice process, seller verification where needed, rate limits, bot protection, account recovery and moderation audit records.

A 'verified garment' badge must mean a documented technical process, not paid status. Separate 3D preview checked, pattern scale checked, measured textile source, simulation contact-checked, manufacturer approved, and community reviewed, each with its own evidence. Never display a generic 'physically accurate' stamp based on a beauty render.

Protect against scraping only to the extent possible; a public view naturally exposes visual design. The material promise is that proprietary source data stays private unless the owner grants it. Each buyer/download receives receipts of license terms, file hashes and seller identity; sellers can see usage summaries without automatically learning buyers' private project files.

Follow controls: chronological feed available, mute/block creators, disable comments, disable remix requests, private bookmarks and opt-out of personalized recommendations. Give creators analytics without exposing individual user behavior beyond what the terms and privacy notice permit.

8. Product phases and realistic build order

GateFuture releaseKey deliverable and acceptance
C0Rights, schema and privacy strategyLicense vocabulary, creator protections, upload threat model, public/private manifests
C1Public visual gallery + profilesAccount, creator portfolio, authorized thumbnails and 3D viewer; private data never transmitted
C2Free pool + downloadsSearchable licensed pattern files, verifiable hashes, format metadata, free entitlements and download receipts
C3Remix lineageFork explicit remixable source only, attribution and dependency tree; incompatible terms block publish
C4Teams and secure project reviewRole-based assets, comments, release snapshots and correct revocation/permission QA
C5Paid marketplaceSeller onboarding, order entitlements, payout/refund accounting, buyer rights and tax/compliance review
C6Virtual wardrobe rooms and studio assetsWalkable user rooms, scene template downloads, source-safe optimized GLB
C7Advanced multiplayer and revenue-share derivativesPresence, optional shared showroom, robust rights splitting and moderation at scale

Do not implement payments before correct entitlements/licensing or a safe uploader. Do not implement collaborative direct CAD edits before reliable version/revision management. Do not start public uploaded PPF processing before job isolation, cost controls and physical validity checks.

Suggested relative effort: profile/gallery prototype 4–8 engineer-weeks once the platform foundation exists; free pool and safe importer 4–8; teams/comments 6–12; paid marketplace 6–12+ plus legal/accounting; remix royalties and large 3D rooms substantially more. These are nonbinding exploratory bands, not calendar estimates or a promise that this is a small side project.

QA success journeys

Creator: signup → private draft hoodie → publish sanitized view-only showcase → another account views geometry but receives no editable size/measurements/edge data → remove it → confirm access revoked. Patternmaker: upload explicitly licensed free pattern with sizing → format/scale validations → stranger downloads with receipt → forks only where remix license allows → original creator linked. Brand team: invite a reviewer and manufacturer into a specific garment release → guest sees approved spec, comments, no confidential formulas → approval history cannot be rewritten. Seller: post paid texture pack → customer receives exact entitled version after charge → revoked link prevents fresh unauthorized download; refund and rights behavior follows explicit store policy. Gallery: 20 mannequin looks in a walkable original room, optimized with LOD, and a list fallback for accessibility.

9. Platform business model: options to evaluate, not decided

Potential revenue streams: low commission on paid digital item sales; optional paid extra storage for large libraries; brand/team seats; optional advanced offline PPF batch processing with transparent compute credits; premium showroom hosting; custom branded pages or manufacturer review seats. Keep a meaningful free tier for public patterns and educational collaboration. Core private drawing rights and safety features should not be withheld for monetization.

Do not assume 'free download' licenses can be flipped to paid without respecting prior rights. Publish versioned product/licensing changes for new customers and preserve historical entitlements. Test economics: median storage per garment, upload conversion costs, image CDN, GPU rendering, payment fees, refund exposure and support/moderation before setting platform pricing.

10. External reference documentation

  • CLO-SET CONNECT Store and uploading/licensing options: marketplace categories and free/paid tiers.
  • CONNECT design-source and component royalties: derivative revenue attribution precedent, not EXO terms.
  • Sketchfab plans: profile previews, embed/annotation and private/unlisted capabilities as documented by vendor.
  • Stripe Connect: marketplace onboarding and payouts.
  • Yjs shared types: candidate for collaborative rich text/comments, not a reason to merge physical CAD geometry without review.
  • OWASP secure file upload: public upload threat model.
  • GitHub license guidance: public viewing alone does not authorize use and derivatives.

Decision: The site should be an independent creator platform over the same intentional asset/garment identity model, with publicly visible fashion work, independently licensed downloadable sources, a huge free pattern pool, optional paid products, accessible walkthrough rooms and robust team/project ACLs. Nothing is uploaded, sold, published or deployed by this research.
